# Haṭha Yoga Pradīpikā (Haṭhapradīpikā)

`src:hatha-yoga-pradipika` · `skeleton` · confidence high

**Alternate titles:** Haṭhapradīpikā, Haṭhayogapradīpikā, HYP
**Original title:** हठयोगप्रदीपिका
**Language:** Sanskrit
**Family:** vedic
**Lineages:** [Haṭha Yoga (the haṭha and rājayoga textual tradition)](../lineages/hatha-yoga.md), [Nāth tradition (Nātha Siddhas; Nāth / Kānphaṭa Yogīs)](../lineages/natha.md)
**Genre:** yoga manual
**Authors:** 
  - [Svātmārāma](../teachers/svatmarama.md) — role: compiler; attribution: accepted
**Attribution:** tradition: by Svātmārāma, who received haṭhavidyā through the grace of Matsyendra, Gorakṣa and the Siddhas (1.4); scholarly: compiled by Svātmārāma c. mid-15th c. from some twenty earlier texts (Amṛtasiddhi, DYŚ, Vivekamārtaṇḍa, Khecarīvidyā, Amaraughaprabodha, Amanaska and others); confidence: high
**Dates:** Tradition's account: in the succession of Ādinātha, Matsyendra and Gorakṣa; Scholarly account: c. 1450 CE; (confidence moderate)
**Structure:** vulgate: 4 upadeśas, c. 389 verses (āsana; prāṇāyāma and ṣaṭkarma; mudrā; samādhi/nādānusandhāna). A longer recension in 10 chapters is also transmitted.
**Availability:** digitized-original

The classic compendium of haṭha, taught 'solely for rājayoga'. Upadeśa 1: lineage, secrecy, the hut, what destroys and promotes yoga, postures, diet. 2: breath-control, the purification of the channels, the six cleansing acts and the eight retentions, with warnings about improper practice. 3: kuṇḍalinī and the ten mudrās (including khecarī and vajrolī). 4: samādhi and its synonyms, the dissolution of mind and breath, śāmbhavī, the four stages and the inner sounds (nādānusandhāna), and the liberated yogin.
**Editions / translations:** 
  - kind: original; name: The Haṭhayogapradīpikā with the Jyotsnā of Brahmānanda (Adyar Library 1893; 1972); licence: unknown
  - kind: original; name: Haṭhapradīpikā, critical edition, Kaivalyadhama (Lonavla 1970; 10-chapter recension 1998); licence: copyright
  - kind: original; name: GRETIL and sanskrit/raw_etexts e-texts; licence: attribution
**Commentaries on this text:** [Jyotsnā (commentary on the Haṭhapradīpikā)](hatha-yoga-pradipika-jyotsna.md)

## Teachings (513: text-verified 393, skeleton 120)

### 1.1 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-1"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Homage to the glorious Ādinātha, by whom the science of haṭhayoga (haṭhayogavidyā) was taught, a science that shines like a stairway for one who wishes to climb to the lofty royal way (rājamārga). The e-text prefixes the verse with the heading 'āsanavidhikathana' (statement of the method of postures).

> āsanavidhikathanaṃ śrīādināthāya namo'stu tasmai yenopadiṣṭā haṭhayogavidyā | vibhrājate pronnatarājamārgamaroḍhumicchannadhirohiṇīva

_level: conventional · standpoint: devotional · path: body-breath, general · stage: all · types: teacher-transmission, practice_

terms: [haṭha](../terms/hatha.md), [rājayoga](../terms/raja-yoga.md) · concepts: [Rājayoga as the goal and its synonyms](../concepts/rajayoga-goal.md), [The Nāth guru-lineage](../concepts/natha-guru-parampara.md) · teachers: [Ādinātha](../teachers/adinatha.md)

### 1.2 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Having bowed to the glorious guru, the Lord (nātha), the yogin Svātmārāma teaches the science of haṭha solely (kevalam) for the sake of rājayoga.

> praṇamya śrīguruṃ nāthaṃ svātmārāmeṇa yoginā | kevalaṃ rājayogāya haṭhavidyopadiśyate

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath, general · stage: all · types: teacher-transmission, practice_

terms: [haṭha](../terms/hatha.md), [rājayoga](../terms/raja-yoga.md) · concepts: [Haṭha and rājayoga depend on each other](../concepts/hatha-raja-interdependence.md), [The guru in haṭha](../concepts/guru-in-hatha.md), [Rājayoga as the goal and its synonyms](../concepts/rajayoga-goal.md) · teachers: [Svātmārāma](../teachers/svatmarama.md)

### 1.3 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-3"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

For those who, through delusion (bhrānti) in the darkness of many conflicting views (bahumata), do not know rājayoga, the compassionate Svātmārāma holds up the 'Lamp of Haṭha' (haṭhapradīpikā).

> bhrāntyā bahumatadhvānte rājayogamajānatām | haṭhapradīpikāṃ dhatte svātmārāmaḥ kṛpākaraḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: all · types: teacher-transmission, practice_

terms: [rājayoga](../terms/raja-yoga.md), [haṭha](../terms/hatha.md) · concepts: [Haṭha and rājayoga depend on each other](../concepts/hatha-raja-interdependence.md), [Rājayoga as the goal and its synonyms](../concepts/rajayoga-goal.md) · teachers: [Svātmārāma](../teachers/svatmarama.md)

### 1.4 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-4"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The science of haṭha is known to Matsyendra, Gorakṣa and others; the yogin Svātmārāma says that he knows it through their grace (prasāda).

> haṭhavidyāṃ hi matsyendragorakṣādyā vijānate | svātmārāmo'thavā yogī janīte tatprasādataḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath, general · stage: all · types: teacher-transmission_

terms: [haṭha](../terms/hatha.md) · teachers: [Matsyendranātha](../teachers/matsyendranatha.md), [Gorakṣanātha](../teachers/goraksanatha.md), [Svātmārāma](../teachers/svatmarama.md)

### 1.4-9 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-4-9"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Matsyendra, Gorakṣa and others know the haṭha knowledge, and by their grace the yogī Svātmārāma; Śrī Ādinātha, Matsyendra, Śābara, Ānandabhairava, Cauraṅgī, Mīna, Gorakṣa, Virūpākṣa, Bileśaya, Manthāna, Bhairava, Siddhi, Buddha, Kanthaḍi, Koraṇṭaka, Surānanda, Siddhapāda, Carpaṭi, Kānerī, Pūjyapāda, Nityanātha, Nirañjana, Kapālī, Bindunātha, Kākacaṇḍīśvara, Allama, Prabhudeva, Ghoḍācolī, Ṭiṇṭiṇi, Bhānukī, Nāradeva, Khaṇḍa, Kāpālika and other great siddhas, having broken the staff of Time by the power of haṭha yoga, roam the universe.

> haṭhavidyāṃ hi matsyendragorakṣādyā vijānate | svātmārāmo'thavā yogī janīte tatprasādataḥ śrīādināthamatsyendraśābarānandabhairavāḥ | cauraṅgīmīnagorakṣavirūpākṣabileśayāḥ manthāno bhairavo yogī siddhirbuddhaśca kanthaḍiḥ | koraṇṭakaḥ surānandaḥ siddhapādaśca carpaṭiḥ kānerī pūjyapādaśca nityanātho nirañjanaḥ | kapalī bindunāthaśca kākacaṇḍīśvarāhvayaḥ allamaḥ prabhudevaśca ghoḍācolī ca ṭiṇṭiṇiḥ | bhānukī nāradevaśca khaṇḍaḥ kāpālikastathā ityādayo mahāsiddhā haṭhayogaprabhāvataḥ | khaṇḍayitvā kāladaṇḍaṃ brahmāṇḍe vicaranti te

### 1.9 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-9"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The text says that these and other great adepts (mahāsiddha), through the power of haṭhayoga, have broken the staff of Death (kāladaṇḍa) and move about in the universe (brahmāṇḍa).

> ityādayo mahāsiddhā haṭhayogaprabhāvataḥ | khaṇḍayitvā kāladaṇḍaṃ brahmāṇḍe vicaranti te

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: all · types: teacher-transmission, karma-liberation_

terms: [haṭha](../terms/hatha.md) · concepts: [The list of great adepts in HYP 1.5-9](../concepts/hyp-mahasiddha-list.md)

### 1.10 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-10"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Haṭha is a sheltering hut (samāśraya-maṭha) for all who are scorched by every kind of affliction (tāpa), and a support (ādhāra) for all who are engaged in every kind of yoga.

> aśeṣatāpataptānāṃ samāśrayamaṭho haṭhaḥ | aśeṣayogayuktānāmādhārakamaṭho haṭhaḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: all · types: practice, karma-liberation_

terms: [haṭha](../terms/hatha.md)

### 1.11 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-11"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The science of haṭha is to be kept utterly secret by the yogin who wishes for accomplishment (siddhi): kept hidden it is potent (vīryavatī), disclosed it is without potency.

> haṭhavidyā paraṃ gopyā yoginā siddhimicchatā | bhaved vīryavatī guptā nirvīryā tu prakāśitā

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath, general · stage: all · types: practice, teacher-transmission_

terms: [haṭha](../terms/hatha.md), [gopya](../terms/gopya.md) · concepts: [Secrecy of haṭha](../concepts/hyp-secrecy.md), [Secrecy and the testing of the disciple](../concepts/secrecy-and-testing.md)

### 1.12 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-12"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The haṭha yogin should live in solitude in a small hut (maṭhikā), in a well-governed, righteous country with plentiful alms (subhikṣa) and free of disturbance, in a place where, for the length of a bow all around (dhanuḥpramāṇaparyantam), there are no stones, fire or water.

> surājye dhārmike deśe subhikṣe nirupadrave | dhanuḥ pramāṇaparyantaṃ śilāgnijalavarjite | ekānte maṭhikāmadhye sthātavyaṃ haṭhayoginā

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: all · types: practice, ethics_

terms: [haṭha](../terms/hatha.md), [maṭha / maṭhikā](../terms/matha.md) · concepts: [The place and hut for yoga](../concepts/yoga-matha.md) · practices: [The yoga-hut (yogamaṭha) and its place](../practices/hyp-yoga-hut.md)

### 1.12-13 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-12-13"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

The haṭha yogin should live alone in a small hut (maṭhikā) in a well-ruled, righteous country where alms are plentiful and there is no disturbance, with no rocks, fire or water within a bow's length. The hut has a small door and no windows, holes or pits; it is neither too high nor too low, well smeared with cow-dung, clean and free of creatures, with a pavilion, raised platform and well outside and a surrounding wall — this is the mark of the yoga-hut as the siddhas who practise haṭha describe it.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: beginner (ārambha (beginning of practice)) · types: practice_

_Superseded by [1.12](hatha-yoga-pradipika.md#tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-12)_

### 1.13 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-13"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The marks of a yoga-hut as stated by the adepts who practise haṭha: a small door; no openings, holes or cracks; neither too high nor too low, and not too long; well smeared with thick cow-dung; clean; entirely free of creatures. Outside, it is pleasing with a pavilion (maṇḍapa), a raised seat (vedī) and a well, and surrounded by an enclosure wall.

> alpadvāramarandhragartavivaraṃ nātyuccanīcāyatam | samyaggomayasāndraliptamamalaṃ niśśeṣajantūjjhitam | bāhye maṇḍapavedikūparuciraṃ prākārasaṃveṣṭitam | proktaṃ yogamaṭhasya lakṣaṇamidaṃ siddhairhaṭhābhyāsibhiḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: all · types: practice_

terms: [maṭha / maṭhikā](../terms/matha.md) · concepts: [The place and hut for yoga](../concepts/yoga-matha.md) · practices: [The yoga-hut (yogamaṭha) and its place](../practices/hyp-yoga-hut.md)

### 1.14 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-14"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Living in such a hut, free from all worries, one should constantly practise yoga alone, by the path taught by the guru.

> evaṃvidhe maṭhe sthitvā sarvacintāvivarjitaḥ | gurūpadiṣṭamārgeṇa yogameva sadābhyaset

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: all (ārambha (beginning of practice)) · types: practice, teacher-transmission_

concepts: [The guru in haṭha](../concepts/guru-in-hatha.md), [The place and hut for yoga](../concepts/yoga-matha.md) · practices: [The yoga-hut (yogamaṭha) and its place](../practices/hyp-yoga-hut.md)

### 1.15 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-15"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Yoga is destroyed by six: overeating (atyāhāra), exertion (prayāsa), idle talk (prajalpa), clinging to rules (niyamagraha), the company of people (janasaṅga), and fickleness/greed (laulya).

> atyāhāraḥ prayāsaśca prajalpo niyamagrahaḥ | janasaṅgaśca laulyaṃ ca ṣaḍbhiryogo vinaśyati

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: all · types: practice, ethics_

concepts: [What destroys and what promotes yoga (HYP 1.15–16)](../concepts/six-destroyers-six-promoters.md) · obstacles: [The six that destroy yoga (HYP 1.15)](../obstacles/hyp-six-destroyers.md), [The six destroyers of yoga (HYP 1.15)](../obstacles/six-destroyers-of-yoga.md), [Overeating (atyāhāra)](../obstacles/atyahara.md), [Overexertion (prayāsa)](../obstacles/prayasa.md), [Too much talk (prajalpa)](../obstacles/prajalpa.md), [Clinging to rules (niyamagraha)](../obstacles/niyamagraha.md), [The company of people (janasaṅga)](../obstacles/janasanga.md), [Unsteadiness / greed (laulya)](../obstacles/laulya.md)

### 1.16 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-16"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Yoga succeeds through six: enthusiasm (utsāha), bold resolve (sāhasa), steadiness (dhairya), knowledge of the truth (tattvajñāna), firm conviction (niścaya), and giving up the company of people (janasaṅgaparityāga).

> utsāhāt sāhasāt dhairyāt tattvajñānācca niścayāt | janasaṅgaparityāgāt ṣaḍbhiryogaḥ prasiddhyati

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: all · types: practice, ethics_

concepts: [The six that further yoga (HYP 1.16)](../concepts/hyp-six-furtherers-of-yoga.md), [What destroys and what promotes yoga (HYP 1.15–16)](../concepts/six-destroyers-six-promoters.md)

### 1.17 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-17"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Now that āsana is the first limb of haṭha, it is taught first. The text says one should practise posture, and that it brings steadiness (sthairya), freedom from disease (ārogya) and lightness of the limbs. In the e-text a bracketed list of ten yamas and ten niyamas stands before this statement (recorded separately as 1.17/2).

> atha yamaniyamāḥ - (ahiṃsā satyamasteyaṃ brahmacaryaṃ kṣamā dhṛtiḥ | dayārjavaṃ mitāhāraḥ śaucaṃ caiva yamā daśa || tapaḥsantoṣa āstikyaṃ dānamīśvarapūjanam | siddhāntavākyaśravaṇaṃ hrīmatī ca japo hutam | niyamā daśa samproktā yogaśāstraviśāradaiḥ | ) haṭhasya prathamāṅgatvadāsanaṃ pūrvamucyate | kuryāt tadāsanaṃ sthairyamārogyaṃ cāṅgalāghavam

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: all (ārambha (beginning of practice)) · types: practice, body-layers_

### 1.17 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-17-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The bracketed list in the e-text: ten yamas (non-harming, truth, non-stealing, continence, forbearance, fortitude, compassion, straightness, moderate eating, purity) and ten niyamas (austerity, contentment, belief in the Veda/God (āstikya), giving, worship of the Lord, hearing the teachings of the tradition, modesty, discernment, muttering (japa), offering (huta)), said to be taught by those expert in yoga treatises.

> ahiṃsā satyamasteyaṃ brahmacaryaṃ kṣamā dhṛtiḥ | dayārjavaṃ mitāhāraḥ śaucaṃ caiva yamā daśa || tapaḥsantoṣa āstikyaṃ dānamīśvarapūjanam | siddhāntavākyaśravaṇaṃ hrīmatī ca japo hutam | niyamā daśa samproktā yogaśāstraviśāradaiḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: all · types: ethics_

Svastikāsana: sitting with the body straight, having placed both soles well between the thighs and the knees.

Gomukhāsana: the ankles set at the opposite sides at the back, right ankle by the left side and left by the right, giving the form of a cow's face.

### 1.26-27 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-26-27"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

With the right foot placed at the root of the left thigh and the left foot wound round outside the knee, holding (the feet) with the body turned — this is the seat taught by Śrī Matsyanātha. Matsyendra's seat kindles the digestive fire, is a weapon that cuts down a host of terrible diseases, and by practice awakens kuṇḍalinī and makes the moon steady.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: intermediate · types: practice, body-layers_

### 1.28-29 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-28-29"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Stretching the legs on the ground like a staff, holding the tips of both feet with the hands and resting the forehead on the knees is called paścimatāna. This foremost posture makes the breath flow along the back (the central channel), kindles the digestive fire, slims the belly and gives health.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: intermediate · types: practice, body-layers_

### 1.29 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-29"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate · _restricted: summary only_

The text praises paścimatāna as the best of postures and makes claims about its effect on breath and digestion.

> iti paścimatānanāmāsanāgryaṃ pavanaṃ paścimavahinaṃ kāroti | udayaṃ jaṭharānalasya kuryādudare kārśyamarogatāṃ ca puṃsām

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: all · types: practice, powers-experiences_

practices: [Paścimatānāsana (the stretch of the back; paścimottāna, ugrāsana)](../practices/pascimatanasana.md)

`text-verified` · confidence moderate · _restricted: summary only_

Describes the posture called māyūra (peacock), in which the body is raised horizontally, supported by the hands.

> dharāmavaṣṭabhya karadvayena tatkūrparasthāpitanābhipārśvaḥ | uccāsano daṇḍavadutthitaḥ khe māyūrametat pravadanti pīṭham

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: all · types: practice_

practices: [Mayūrāsana (the peacock)](../practices/mayurasana.md)

### 1.30-31 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-30-31"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Pressing the ground with both hands, setting the sides of the navel on the elbows and rising high into the air like a staff — this is called the peacock seat. It quickly removes diseases such as gulma and abdominal swellings, overcomes the doṣas, reduces to ash all bad food eaten, kindles the digestive fire and digests even the deadly poison kālakūṭa.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: intermediate · types: practice, body-layers_

practices: [Mayūrāsana (the peacock)](../practices/mayurasana.md)

_Superseded by [1.30](hatha-yoga-pradipika.md#tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-30)_

`text-verified` · confidence moderate · _restricted: summary only_

Praises mayūrāsana with strong claims (that it removes diseases and faulty humours, digests badly eaten food and even a deadly poison).

Śavāsana: lying flat on the back on the ground like a corpse. The text says it removes fatigue (śrānti) and brings the mind to rest (cittaviśrānti).

> uttānaṃ śavavadbhūmau śayanaṃ tacchavāsanam | śavāsanaṃ śrāntiharaṃ cittaviśrāntikārakam

### 1.33 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-33"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Śiva taught eighty-four postures; from them the speaker will state a set of four as the essential ones.

> caturaśītyāsanāni śivena kathitāni ca | tebhyaścatuṣkamādāya sārabhūtaṃ bravīmyaham

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: all · types: practice, teacher-transmission_

concepts: [The eighty-four postures taught by Śiva (HYP)](../concepts/eighty-four-asanas.md), [The four chief postures (siddha, padma, siṃha, bhadra)](../concepts/hyp-four-chief-asanas.md)

### 1.33-34 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-33-34"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Śiva taught eighty-four postures; of these four are the essence — siddha, padma, siṃha and bhadra — and of these the best is siddhāsana, in which one should always sit.

> caturaśītyāsanāni śivena kathitāni ca | tebhyaścatuṣkamādāya sārabhūtaṃ bravīmyaham || siddhaṃ padmaṃ tathā siṃhaṃ bhadraṃ ceti catuṣṭayam | śreṣṭhaṃ tatrāpi ca sukhe tiṣṭhet siddhāsane sadā

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: all (ārambha (beginning of practice)) · types: practice_

concepts: [The eighty-four postures taught by Śiva (HYP)](../concepts/eighty-four-asanas.md) · practices: [Siddhāsana (the adept's seat)](../practices/siddhasana.md), [Padmāsana (the lotus seat)](../practices/padmasana.md), [Siṃhāsana (the lion seat)](../practices/simhasana.md), [Bhadrāsana (the auspicious seat)](../practices/bhadrasana.md) · teachers: [Ādinātha](../teachers/adinatha.md)

_Superseded by [1.33](hatha-yoga-pradipika.md#tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-33)_

### 1.34 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-34"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The four are siddha, padma, siṃha and bhadra. Among these too, the best: one should always sit at ease in siddhāsana.

> siddhaṃ padmaṃ tathā siṃhaṃ bhadraṃ ceti catuṣṭayam | śreṣṭhaṃ tatrāpi ca sukhe tiṣṭhet siddhāsane sadā

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: all · types: practice_

concepts: [The four chief postures (siddha, padma, siṃha, bhadra)](../concepts/hyp-four-chief-asanas.md) · practices: [Siddhāsana (the adept's seat)](../practices/siddhasana.md)

### 1.35 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-35"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Siddhāsana: seated with one heel placed firmly at the base of the trunk (yonisthāna) and the other foot above the genitals, the chin set steady on the chest, the body still, the senses restrained and the gaze fixed unmoving between the brows. The text says this is what breaks open the door of liberation.

> yonisthānakamaṅghrimūlaghaṭitaṃ kṛtvā dṛḍhaṃ vinyaset meḍhre pādamathaikameva hṛdaye kṛtvā hanuṃ susthiram | sthāṇussaṃyamitendriyo'caladṛśā paśyed bhruvorantaram hyetanmokṣakapāṭabhedajanakaṃ siddhāsanaṃ procyate

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: all · types: practice_

practices: [Siddhāsana (the adept's seat)](../practices/siddhasana.md)

### 1.35-36 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-35-36"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Siddhāsana: pressing the perineum (yonisthāna) firmly with the heel of one foot, placing the other foot above the penis, setting the chin firmly on the chest, still, the senses restrained, the gaze unmoving between the brows — this opens the door of liberation. In another way the left ankle is placed above the penis and the other ankle above it.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: beginner (ārambha (beginning of practice)) · types: practice_

practices: [Siddhāsana (the adept's seat)](../practices/siddhasana.md)

### 1.36 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-36"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Another statement of siddhāsana: the left ankle placed above the genitals and the other ankle placed above it.

> meḍhrādupari vinyasya savyaṃ gulphaṃ tathopari | gulphāntaraṃ ca nikṣipya siddhāsanamidaṃ bhavet

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: all · types: practice_

practices: [Siddhāsana (the adept's seat)](../practices/siddhasana.md)

### 1.37 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-37"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Some call this siddhāsana vajrāsana; some say muktāsana; others call it guptāsana.

> etatsiddhāsanaṃ prāhuranye vajrāsanaṃ viduḥ | muktāsanaṃ vadantyeke prāhurguptāsanaṃ pare

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: all (ārambha (beginning of practice)) · types: practice_

### 1.38 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-38"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

As moderate eating is chief among the yamas and non-harming among the niyamas, so the adepts know siddhāsana as the one chief among all postures.

> yameṣviva mitāhāramahiṃsāṃ niyameṣviva | mukhyaṃ sarvāsaneṣvekaṃ siddhāḥ siddhāsanaṃ viduḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: all · types: practice, ethics_

terms: [mitāhāra](../terms/mitahara.md) · practices: [Siddhāsana (the adept's seat)](../practices/siddhasana.md)

### 1.38-39 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-38-39"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

As moderate diet among the restraints and non-harming among the observances, so siddhāsana is the chief of all postures, say the Siddhas; among the eighty-four seats one should always practise siddhāsana, which cleanses the impurities of the seventy-two thousand channels.

> yameṣviva mitāhāramahiṃsāṃ niyameṣviva | mukhyaṃ sarvāsaneṣvekaṃ siddhāḥ siddhāsanaṃ viduḥ || caturaśītipīṭheṣu siddhameva sadābhyaset | dvāsaptatisahasrāṇāṃ nāḍīnāṃ malaśodhanam

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: all · types: practice, body-layers_

terms: [nāḍī](../terms/nadi.md) · concepts: [The channels (nāḍī)](../concepts/nadis.md) · practices: [Siddhāsana (the adept's seat)](../practices/siddhasana.md)

_Superseded by [1.38](hatha-yoga-pradipika.md#tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-38)_

### 1.38-40 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-38-40"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

As moderate diet is chief among the restraints and non-harm among the observances, the siddhas know siddhāsana as chief among postures. Among the eighty-four seats one should always practise siddha; it cleanses the impurity of the seventy-two thousand channels. Meditating on the self, eating moderately, the yogin who practises siddhāsana for twelve years attains completion (niṣpatti).

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: beginner (ārambha (beginning of practice)) · types: practice, body-layers_

practices: [Siddhāsana (the adept's seat)](../practices/siddhasana.md)

_Superseded by [1.38](hatha-yoga-pradipika.md#tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-38)_

### 1.39 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-39"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Among the eighty-four seats one should always practise siddhāsana alone; the text says it purifies the impurities of the seventy-two thousand channels (nāḍī).

> caturaśītipīṭheṣu siddhameva sadābhyaset | dvāsaptatisahasrāṇāṃ nāḍīnāṃ malaśodhanam

### 1.40 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-40"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate · _restricted: summary only_

The text says that the yogin who meditates on the self (ātmadhyāyī), eats moderately (mitāhārī) and constantly practises siddhāsana attains completion (niṣpatti); the period the verse names is not reproduced here.

> ātmadhyāyī mitāhārī yāvad-dvādaśavatsaram | sadā siddhāsanābhyāsāt yogī niṣpattimāpnuyāt

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: all · types: practice_

### 1.41 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-41"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate · _restricted: summary only_

The text asks what use many other seats are once siddhāsana is accomplished, and says that when the breath (prāṇānila) is held attentively, bound in kevala kumbhaka, the state called unmanī arises of itself, without effort. Kevala kumbhaka is named only; no method is given.

> kimanyairbahubhiḥ pīṭhaiḥ siddhe siddhāsane sati | prāṇānile sāvadhāne baddhe kevalakumbhake | utpadyate nirāyāsāt svayamevonmanī kalā

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: all · types: practice_

terms: [kumbhaka](../terms/kumbhaka.md), [unmanī](../terms/unmani.md) · practices: [Siddhāsana (the adept's seat)](../practices/siddhasana.md)

### 1.41-42 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-41-42"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

When siddhāsana is mastered, what use are many other seats? When the breath is carefully bound in kevala kumbhaka the unmanī state arises of itself without effort; and in that one firm posture the three locks arise spontaneously.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: advanced · types: practice, consciousness-mind_

practices: [Siddhāsana (the adept's seat)](../practices/siddhasana.md), [Kevala-kumbhaka (retention alone)](../practices/kevala-kumbhaka.md), [The three locks together (bandha-traya)](../practices/bandha-traya.md)

`text-verified` · confidence moderate · _restricted: summary only_

Padmāsana, in its bound form, with the feet placed on the opposite thighs, the chin drawn to the chest and the gaze on the nose-tip; the text says it destroys the diseases of the self-controlled.

> atha padmāsanam - vāmorūpari dakṣiṇaṃ ca caraṇaṃ saṃsthāpya vāmaṃ tathā dakṣorūpari paścimena vidhinā dhṛtvā karābhyāṃ dṛḍham | aṅguṣṭhau hṛdaye nidhāya cibukaṃ nāsāgramālokayed etadvyādhivināśahāri yamināṃ padmāsanaṃ procyate

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: all (ārambha (beginning of practice)) · types: practice_

`skeleton` · confidence high

In another view: the feet set soles-up on the thighs, the hands palms-up between the thighs, the gaze on the nose-tip, the tongue pressed at the root of the front teeth, the chin on the chest, raising the breath slowly — this padmāsana destroys all diseases; it is hard to attain and only the wise obtain it.

Foods declared unwholesome for the yogin (pungent, sour, sharp, salty and hot foods, certain greens, sesame and mustard, alcohol, fish, meat, curd, buttermilk and others; reheated, dry, over-salted food); at the start one should avoid fire, women and travel — as Gorakṣa said: avoid the company of the wicked, fire, women, travel, early-morning bathing, fasting and practices that torment the body.

### 1.65 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-65"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Accomplishment (siddhi) comes to one engaged in practice (kriyā); how could it come to one who is inactive? Yoga-siddhi does not arise by mere reading of the scriptures.

> kriyāyuktasya siddhiḥ syādakriyasya kathaṃ bhavet | na śāstrapāṭhamātreṇa yogasiddhiḥ prajāyate

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: all · types: practice, ethics_

concepts: [Practice, not talk, dress or scripture-reading](../concepts/hyp-practice-not-talk.md)

### 1.65-66 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-65-66"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Success comes to one who practises; how could it come to one who does not? Success in yoga does not arise from merely reading scriptures, nor is wearing the garb (of an ascetic) or talking about it the cause of success: practice alone is the cause — this is the truth.

> kriyāyuktasya siddhiḥ syādakriyasya kathaṃ bhavet | na śāstrapāṭhamātreṇa yogasiddhiḥ prajāyate || na veṣadhāraṇaṃ siddheḥ kāraṇaṃ na ca tatkathā | kriyaiva kāraṇaṃ siddheḥ satyametanna saṃśayaḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: polemical · path: body-breath · stage: all · types: practice, ethics_

concepts: [Practice, not garb or learning, is the cause of success](../concepts/practice-not-garb.md) · obstacles: [Hypocrisy of the unrealised (dambha)](../obstacles/dambha.md)

_Superseded by [1.65](hatha-yoga-pradipika.md#tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-65)_

### 1.66 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-1-66"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The wearing of a distinctive garb is not the cause of siddhi, nor is talk about it; practice alone is the cause of siddhi. 'This is true; there is no doubt.'

> na veṣadhāraṇaṃ siddheḥ kāraṇaṃ na ca tatkathā | kriyaiva kāraṇaṃ siddheḥ satyametanna saṃśayaḥ

`text-verified` · confidence high

Postures, the varied retentions and the divine practices (karaṇa): all of these belong to haṭha practice, up to the fruit of rājayoga (rājayogaphalāvadhi).

> pīṭhāni kumbhakāścitrā divyāni karaṇāni ca sarvāṇyapi haṭhābhyāse rājayogaphālāvadhi

`text-verified` · confidence moderate · _restricted: summary only_

Second upadeśa. Prāṇāyāma is the subject: when posture is firm, the self-controlled yogin of regulated diet should practise the prāṇāyāmas by the path taught by the guru; summary only.

> dvitīyopadeśaḥ athāsane dṛḍhe yogī vaśī hitamitāśanaḥ | gurūpadiṣṭamārgeṇa prāṇāyāmān samabhyaset

### 2.2 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high · _restricted: summary only_

The text gives its reason for breath-control: when the breath moves the mind moves, when the breath is still the mind is still, and the yogin attains steadiness.

> cale vāte calaṃ cittaṃ niścale niścalaṃ bhavet | yogī sthāṇutvamāpnoti tato vāyuṃ nirodhayet

### 2.3 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-3"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high · _restricted: summary only_

The text says that life lasts as long as the breath stays in the body, and death is its departure; so the breath is to be restrained.

### 2.4 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-4"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high · _restricted: summary only_

The text says that when the channels (nāḍī) are full of impurities the breath does not go in the middle; how then could the state of unmanī arise, or the work be accomplished?

> malākulāsu nāḍīṣu māruto naiva madhyagaḥ | kathaṃ syādunmanībhāvaḥ kāryasiddhiḥ kathaṃ bhavet

### 2.4-5 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-4-5"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

When the channels are full of impurities the breath does not go into the middle; how then can the state of unmanī arise or the goal be reached? Only when the whole network of channels, full of impurities, is purified does the yogin become able to hold the breath.

> malākulāsu nāḍīṣu māruto naiva madhyagaḥ | kathaṃ syādunmanībhāvaḥ kāryasiddhiḥ kathaṃ bhavet || śuddhimeti yadā sarvaṃ nāḍīcakraṃ malākulam | tadaiva jāyate yogī prāṇasaṅgrahaṇe kṣamaḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: body-breath · stage: all · types: body-layers, practice_

terms: [nāḍī](../terms/nadi.md), [unmanī](../terms/unmani.md), [nāḍīśuddhi](../terms/nadi-suddhi.md) · concepts: [The channels (nāḍī)](../concepts/nadis.md), [Suṣumnā, the central channel (HYP)](../concepts/susumna-central-channel.md), [Unmanī / manonmanī — the state beyond mind](../concepts/unmani.md) · obstacles: [Impurities in the channels](../obstacles/malas-in-nadis.md)

### 2.4-6 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-4-6"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

While the channels are full of impurity the breath does not go into the middle; how then can the unmanī state or success arise? When the whole wheel of channels, full of impurity, is purified, then the yogin becomes able to hold the breath; so one should practise breath-control daily with a sāttvika mind, so that the impurities lodged in the suṣumnā are cleared.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: intermediate · types: practice, body-layers_

### 2.5 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-5"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high · _restricted: summary only_

When the whole circle of channels (nāḍīcakra) that was full of impurities has become pure, then the yogin is able to take hold of the breath (prāṇasaṅgrahaṇa).

> śuddhimeti yadā sarvaṃ nāḍīcakraṃ malākulam | tadaiva jāyate yogī prāṇasaṅgrahaṇe kṣamaḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: unmarked · types: practice, body-layers_

### 2.6 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-6"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate · _restricted: summary only_

Prāṇāyāma is to be practised constantly with a pure (sāttvika) mind so that the impurities in the channel of suṣumnā are purified; summary only.

> prāṇayāmaṃ tataḥ kuryānnityaṃ sātvikayā dhiyā | yathā suṣumnānāḍīsthā malāḥ śuddhiṃ prayānti ca

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: unmarked · types: practice_

terms: [prāṇāyāma](../terms/pranayama.md), [suṣumnā](../terms/susumna.md), [nāḍī](../terms/nadi.md)

### 2.6-10 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-6-10"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Therefore one should practise breath-control daily with a sattvic mind, so that the impurities in the central channel are purified: seated in padmāsana, breathe in through the moon (left) channel, hold as long as one can and breathe out through the sun (right); then in through the right and out through the left — always exhaling through the channel other than the one used for inhalation, slowly and not forcefully; by this practice with sun and moon the channels of the self-controlled become pure.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: beginner · types: practice, body-layers_

terms: [iḍā](../terms/ida.md), [piṅgalā](../terms/pingala.md), [nāḍīśuddhi](../terms/nadi-suddhi.md) · concepts: [Sun, moon and fire in the body](../concepts/sun-moon-fire.md) · practices: [Purification of the channels (nāḍī-śodhana, nāḍī-śuddhi)](../practices/nadi-sodhana.md)

_Superseded by [2.6](hatha-yoga-pradipika.md#tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-6)_

### 2.7 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-7"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate · _restricted: summary only_

Begins the account of a breath-practice for purifying the channels, done through the two sides (moon and sun); summary only, no method recorded.

> baddhapadmāsano yogī prāṇaṃ candreṇa pūrayet | dhārayitvā yathāśakti bhūyaḥ sūryeṇa recayet

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: unmarked · types: practice_

terms: [nāḍī](../terms/nadi.md), [kumbhaka](../terms/kumbhaka.md) · practices: [Purification of the channels by breath (HYP 2.7-10)](../practices/hyp-nadi-shodhana-summary.md)

### 2.7-10 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-7-10"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Purification of the channels: seated in bound padmāsana, draw the breath in through the moon (left nostril), hold it as far as one is able, and release it through the sun (right); then draw in through the sun, fill the belly slowly, hold it by rule and release through the moon. Inhale through the nostril by which one exhaled; release slowly, never with force. For the self-restrained who practise constantly in this way with sun and moon, the channels become pure after three months.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: intermediate · types: practice_

practices: [Purification of the channels (nāḍī-śodhana, nāḍī-śuddhi)](../practices/nadi-sodhana.md)

_Superseded by [2.7](hatha-yoga-pradipika.md#tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-7)_

`text-verified` · confidence moderate · _restricted: summary only_

### 2.22-23 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-22-23"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

The six acts are dhauti, basti, neti, trāṭaka, nauli and kapālabhāti; this set of six, which purifies the body (ghaṭa) and yields wonderful results, is to be kept secret and is honoured by the best of yogins.

> dhautirvastiḥ tathā netiḥ trāṭakaṃ naulikaṃ tathā | kapālabhātiścaitāni ṣaṭkarmāṇi pracakṣate || karmaṣaṭkamidaṃ gopyaṃ ghaṭaśodhanakārakam | vicitraguṇasandhāyi pūjyate yogipuṅgavaiḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: all · types: practice_

Practised, water-basti clarifies the bodily tissues, senses and mind, gives lustre, kindles the fire and destroys all accumulated disorders.

### 2.36-37 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-36-37"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Freed of obesity, phlegm and impurities by the six acts, one should then practise breath-control, which then succeeds without strain. Some teachers hold that all impurities dry up through breath-control alone and do not approve of the other acts.

> ṣaṭkarmanirgatasthaulyakaphadoṣamalādikaḥ | prāṇāyāmaṃ tataḥ kuryādanāyāsena siddhyati || prāṇāyāmaireva sarve praśuṣyanti malā iti | ācāryāṇāntu keṣāñcidanyat karma na sammatam

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: unmarked · types: practice, death-dying_

### 2.39-40 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-39-40"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Even Brahmā and the gods, from fear of death, were devoted to the practice of breath; therefore practise the breath. As long as the breath is bound in the body, the mind undisturbed and the gaze between the brows, whence the fear of death?

> brahmādayo'pi tridaśā pavanābhyāsatatparāḥ | abhūvannantakabhayāt tasmāt pavanamabhyaset || yāvad baddho maruddehe yāvaccittaṃ nirākulam | yāvad-dṛṣṭiḥ bhruvormadhye tāvat kālabhayaṃ kutaḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: body-breath · stage: all · types: death-dying, practice_

concepts: [Cheating time (kāla-vañcana)](../concepts/kala-vancana.md)

_Superseded by [2.39](hatha-yoga-pradipika.md#tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-39)_

### 2.40 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-40"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate · _restricted: summary only_

The text says that as long as the breath is bound in the body, the mind is undisturbed and the gaze is between the brows, there is no fear of time (death).

`text-verified` · confidence moderate · _restricted: summary only_

The text says that when the circle of channels has been purified by breath-control done according to rule (vidhivat), the breath enters the mouth of suṣumnā with ease.

> vidhivatprāṇasaṃyāmaiḥ nāḍīcakre viśodhite | suṣumnāvadanaṃ bhittvā sukhād viśati mārutaḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: unmarked · types: practice, body-layers_

### 2.41-42 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-41-42"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

When the channels are purified by breath-control done according to rule, the breath pierces the mouth of suṣumnā and enters it easily; when the breath moves in the middle, the mind becomes steady; that steadiness of mind is the state of manonmanī.

> vidhivatprāṇasaṃyāmaiḥ nāḍīcakre viśodhite | suṣumnāvadanaṃ bhittvā sukhād viśati mārutaḥ || mārute madhyasañcāre manaḥsthairyaṃ prajāyate | yo manaḥsusthirībhāvaḥ saivāvasthā manonmanī

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: body-breath, meditation · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind, body-layers, practice_

terms: [suṣumnā](../terms/susumna.md), [manonmanī](../terms/manonmani.md) · concepts: [Suṣumnā, the central channel (HYP)](../concepts/susumna-central-channel.md), [Unmanī / manonmanī — the state beyond mind](../concepts/unmani.md), [The bond of mind and breath](../concepts/mind-breath-bond.md)

_Superseded by [2.41](hatha-yoga-pradipika.md#tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-41)_

### 2.42 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-42"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high · _restricted: summary only_

The text says that when the breath moves in the middle, steadiness of mind arises, and that this steady state of mind is the condition called manonmanī.

### 2.45-46 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-45-46"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

At the end of inhalation the lock called jālandhara is applied; at the end of retention, before exhalation, uḍḍiyāna; with the lower contraction and the throat contraction, and the drawing-in of the middle, the breath goes into the channel of Brahman.

> pūrakānte tu kartavyo bandho jālandharābhidhaḥ | kumbhakānte recakadau kartavyastūḍḍiyanakaḥ || adhastāt kuñcanenāśu kaṇṭhasaṅkocane kṛte | madhye paścimatānena syāt prāṇo brahmanāḍigaḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: advanced · types: practice, body-layers_

terms: [bandha](../terms/bandha.md), [brahmanāḍī](../terms/brahma-nadi.md) · practices: [Jālandharabandha (the throat lock)](../practices/jalandhara-bandha.md), [Uḍḍīyānabandha (the flying-up lock)](../practices/uddiyana-bandha.md), [Mūlabandha (the root lock)](../practices/mula-bandha.md)

### 2.45-47 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-45-47"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

At the end of inhalation the lock called jālandhara is applied; at the end of retention and the beginning of exhalation, uḍḍiyāna. With the contraction below, the throat contracted and the middle drawn back, the breath goes into the brahma-channel. Raising the apāna and bringing the prāṇa down from the throat, the yogin, freed from old age, becomes like one of sixteen years.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: advanced · types: practice, body-layers_

practices: [Jālandharabandha (the throat lock)](../practices/jalandhara-bandha.md), [Uḍḍīyānabandha (the flying-up lock)](../practices/uddiyana-bandha.md), [Mūlabandha (the root lock)](../practices/mula-bandha.md), [The three locks together (bandha-traya)](../practices/bandha-traya.md)

_Superseded by [2.45](hatha-yoga-pradipika.md#tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-45)_

### 2.59-67 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-59-67"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Bhastrikā: seated well in padmāsana with neck and belly aligned, the mouth closed, one exhales forcefully through the nose so that the breath sounds in the heart, throat and skull, and inhales forcefully to the heart-lotus, again and again, moving the breath in one's own body as a blacksmith works his bellows; when tired, one fills through the sun (right), holds firmly with the nostrils closed by the fingers, and releases through iḍā. It removes vāta, pitta and phlegm, increases the bodily fire, quickly awakens kuṇḍalinī, destroys the phlegm that bars the mouth of the brahma-channel and pierces the three knots; it should be practised especially.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: advanced · types: practice, body-layers_

practices: [Bhastrikā ('the bellows')](../practices/bhastrika.md)

_Superseded by [2.59](hatha-yoga-pradipika.md#tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-59)_

### 2.71-72 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-71-72"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Breath-control is threefold — exhalation, inhalation and retention; retention is of two kinds, sahita ('accompanied') and kevala ('alone'); one should practise sahita until kevala is attained.

> prāṇāyāmastridhā prokto recapūrakakumbhakaiḥ | sahitaḥ kevalaśceti kumbhako dvividho mataḥ || yāvat kevalasiddhiḥ syāt sahitaṃ tāvadabhyaset | recakaṃ pūrakaṃ muktvā sukhaṃ yad vāyudhāraṇam

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: body-breath · stage: all · types: practice_

terms: [kumbhaka](../terms/kumbhaka.md), [recaka](../terms/recaka.md), [pūraka](../terms/puraka.md), [kevala kumbhaka](../terms/kevala-kumbhaka.md) · practices: [Sahita-kumbhaka (retention joined with inhalation and exhalation)](../practices/sahita-kumbhaka.md), [Kevala-kumbhaka (retention alone)](../practices/kevala-kumbhaka.md)

_Superseded by [2.71](hatha-yoga-pradipika.md#tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-71)_

### 2.72 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-72"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate · _restricted: summary only_

The text says the sahita form is to be practised until the kevala form is accomplished; summary only.

> yāvat kevalasiddhiḥ syāt sahitaṃ tāvadabhyaset | recakaṃ pūrakaṃ muktvā sukhaṃ yad vāyudhāraṇam

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: unmarked · types: practice_

terms: [kumbhaka](../terms/kumbhaka.md)

### 2.72-74 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-72-74"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

One should practise sahita until kevala is mastered. The easy holding of the breath free of exhalation and inhalation is the breath-control called kevala kumbhaka. When kevala retention, free of exhalation and inhalation, is mastered, nothing in the three worlds is hard to attain for one able to hold the breath at will.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: advanced · types: practice, powers-experiences_

practices: [Kevala-kumbhaka (retention alone)](../practices/kevala-kumbhaka.md), [Sahita-kumbhaka (retention joined with inhalation and exhalation)](../practices/sahita-kumbhaka.md)

_Superseded by [2.72](hatha-yoga-pradipika.md#tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-72)_

### 2.73 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-73"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate · _restricted: summary only_

The text says that kevala kumbhaka is the retention that arises without inhalation or exhalation; summary only.

> prāṇāyāmo'yamityuktaḥ sa vai kevalakumbhakaḥ | kumbhake kevale siddhe recapūrakavarjite

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: unmarked · types: practice_

terms: [kumbhaka](../terms/kumbhaka.md)

### 2.73-75 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-73-75"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

When kevala retention, free of exhalation and inhalation, is mastered, nothing in the three worlds is unattainable; one able to hold the breath at will by kevala attains even the state of rājayoga. From retention comes the awakening of kuṇḍalinī; from that, suṣumnā becomes free of obstruction and haṭha is perfected.

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: body-breath · stage: advanced · types: practice, powers-experiences_

terms: [kevala kumbhaka](../terms/kevala-kumbhaka.md), [kuṇḍalinī](../terms/kundalini.md) · concepts: [Kuṇḍalinī](../concepts/kundalini.md), [Suṣumnā, the central channel (HYP)](../concepts/susumna-central-channel.md), [Rājayoga as the goal and its synonyms](../concepts/rajayoga-goal.md) · practices: [Kevala-kumbhaka (retention alone)](../practices/kevala-kumbhaka.md)

_Superseded by [2.73](hatha-yoga-pradipika.md#tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-73)_

### 2.74 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-74"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate · _restricted: summary only_

The text claims that when kevala kumbhaka is accomplished nothing in the three worlds is hard to obtain for the yogin, who can hold the breath at will; claim not reproduced as fact.

> na tasya durlabhaṃ kiñcit triṣu lokeṣu vidyate | śaktaḥ kevalakumbhena yatheṣṭaṃ vāyudhāraṇam

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: unmarked · types: practice, powers-experiences_

### 2.75 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-75"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate · _restricted: summary only_

The text says that from this retention he attains the state of rājayoga and that kuṇḍalī is awakened, the suṣumnā becomes unobstructed and haṭha's accomplishment arises.

### 2.76 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-76"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Haṭha without rājayoga, and rājayoga without haṭha, do not succeed; therefore one should practise the pair until the work is complete (niṣpatti).

> haṭhaṃ vinā rājayogo rājayogaṃ vinā haṭhaḥ | na sidhyati tato yugmamamāniṣpatteḥ samabhyaset

`text-verified` · confidence moderate · _restricted: summary only_

The text says that at the end of the breath-stopping the mind is to be made without support (nirāśraya), and that by such practice one reaches the state of rājayoga; summary only for the breath practice.

### 2.78 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-2-78"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate · _restricted: summary only_

The text lists the marks of success in haṭha (haṭhasiddhi): a lean body, a serene face, a distinct inner sound, very clear eyes, freedom from disease, mastery of bindu, kindled digestive fire and purified channels.

> vapuḥ kṛśatvaṃ vadane prasannatā nādasphuṭatvaṃ nayane sunirmale | arogatā bindujayo'gnidīpanaṃ nāḍīviśuddhiḥ haṭhasiddhilakṣaṇam

### 3.1 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-3-1"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Third upadeśa. As the lord of serpents supports the earth with its mountains and forests, so kuṇḍalī is the support of all the yoga-tantras.

> mudrāvidhānaṃ saśailavanadhātrīṇāṃ yathādharo'hināyakaḥ | sarveṣāṃ yogatantrāṇāṃ tathādhāro hi kuṇḍalī

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: body-breath · stage: all · types: body-layers, practice_

### 3.2 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-3-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

When kuṇḍalī, who sleeps, is awakened by the guru's grace, then all the lotuses (padma) and the knots (granthi) are pierced.

> suptā guruprasādena yadā jagarti kuṇḍalī | tadā sarvāṇi padmāni bhidyante granthayo'pi ca

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: body-breath · stage: all · types: body-layers, powers-experiences_

terms: [kuṇḍalinī](../terms/kundalini.md) · concepts: [The guru in haṭha](../concepts/guru-in-hatha.md)

### 3.2-3 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-3-2-3"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

When the sleeping kuṇḍalī awakens by the grace of the guru, all the lotuses and knots are pierced; the breath's path through the void becomes a royal road, the mind becomes supportless, and time is cheated.

> suptā guruprasādena yadā jagarti kuṇḍalī | tadā sarvāṇi padmāni bhidyante granthayo'pi ca prāṇasya śūnyapadavī tadā rājapathāyate | tadā cittaṃ nirālambaṃ tadā kālasya vañcanam

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: body-breath, meditation · stage: advanced · types: body-layers, powers-experiences, death-dying_

`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The text says that then the void path (śūnyapadavī) of the breath becomes like a royal road, the mind is without support (nirālamba), and there is the cheating of Death (kālasya vañcanam).

> prāṇasya śūnyapadavī tadā rājapathāyate | tadā cittaṃ nirālambaṃ tadā kālasya vañcanam

### 3.4 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-3-4"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Suṣumnā, the void path (śūnyapadavī), the aperture of Brahman (brahmarandhra), the great path (mahāpatha), the cremation ground (śmaśāna), śāmbhavī and the middle way (madhyamārga) are all names for one thing.

> suṣumnā śūnyapadavī brahmarandhraṃ mahāpathaḥ | śmaśānaṃ śāmbhavī madhyamārgaścetyekavācakāḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: body-breath · stage: all · types: body-layers, sound-language_

terms: [suṣumnā](../terms/susumna.md), [brahmarandhra](../terms/brahmarandhra.md) · concepts: [Suṣumnā, the central channel (HYP)](../concepts/susumna-central-channel.md)

### 3.4-5 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-3-4-5"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Suṣumnā, the path of the void, the brahmarandhra, the great way, the cremation ground, śāmbhavī and the middle path are synonyms. Therefore one should practise the mudrās with every effort to awaken the goddess asleep at the mouth of Brahmā's door.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: advanced · types: body-layers, practice_

terms: [suṣumnā](../terms/susumna.md)

### 3.5 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-3-5"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate · _restricted: summary only_

Therefore, the text says, with every effort mudrā practice is to be done to awaken the Lord (īśvara, i.e. kuṇḍalī) sleeping at the mouth of the door of Brahman; summary only.

> tasmāt sarvaprayatnena prabodhayitumīśvaram | brahmadvāramukhe suptāṃ mudrābhyāsaṃ samācaret

> mahāmudrā mahabandho mahāvedhaśca khecarī | uḍyānaṃ mūlabandhaśca bandho jālandharābhidhaḥ

`skeleton` · confidence high

The ten mudrās — mahāmudrā, mahābandha, mahāvedha, khecarī, uḍḍiyāna, mūlabandha, jālandhara, viparītakaraṇī, vajrolī and śakticālana — destroy old age and death.

> mahāmudrā mahabandho mahāvedhaśca khecarī | uḍyānaṃ mūlabandhaśca bandho jālandharābhidhaḥ || karaṇī viparītākhyā vajrolī śakticālanam | idaṃ hi mudrādaśakaṃ jarāmaraṇanāśakam

`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The text says the ten mudrās were taught by Ādinātha, are divine, give the eight lordly powers (aṣṭaiśvarya), are dear to all the adepts and are hard to obtain even for the gods (maruts).

### 3.8-9 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-3-8-9"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Taught by Ādinātha, they bestow the eight powers, are dear to all the Siddhas and hard even for the gods to obtain; they must be guarded with effort like a box of jewels and told to no one, just as a woman of good family does not speak of her lovemaking.

> ādināthoditaṃ divyamaṣṭaiśvaryapradāyakam | vallabhaṃ sarvasiddhānāṃ durlabhaṃ marutāmapi || gopanīyaṃ prayatnena yathā ratnakaraṇḍakam | kasyacinnaiva vaiktavyaṃ kulastrī surataṃ yathā

`text-verified` · confidence high

The text's injunction of secrecy: they are to be guarded with effort like a casket of jewels and told to no one, as a well-born wife guards intimacy.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: unmarked · types: practice_

### 3.10-13 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-3-10-13"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Mahāmudrā: pressing the perineum (yoni) with the left heel and stretching out the right leg, holding (the foot) firmly with the hands, applying the throat lock and holding the breath upward, the power kuṇḍalinī suddenly becomes straight, like a snake struck with a stick becomes stick-like; then a death-like state arises, the (breath) leaving the two channels. Then one exhales slowly, never forcefully. This is mahāmudrā, shown by the great siddhas.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: advanced · types: practice, powers-experiences_

practices: [Mahāmudrā (the great seal)](../practices/mahamudra.md)

_Superseded by [3.10](hatha-yoga-pradipika.md#tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-3-10)_

### 3.11 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-3-11"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate · _restricted: summary only_

Continues mahāmudrā; the text compares the result to a serpent struck by a staff that becomes straight; summary only, no method.

> kaṇṭhe bandhaṃ samāropya dhārayed vāyumūrdhvataḥ | yathā daṇḍahataḥ sarpaḥ daṇḍākāraḥ prajāyate

`skeleton` · confidence high

(In mahāmudrā) as a snake struck with a stick becomes straight like a stick, so kuṇḍalinī śakti suddenly becomes straight; then the state of death arises, 'resting on the two channels' (dvipuṭāśrayā).

> kaṇṭhe bandhaṃ samāropya dhārayed vāyumūrdhvataḥ | yathā daṇḍahataḥ sarpaḥ daṇḍākāraḥ prajāyate || ṛjvībhūtā tathā śaktiḥ kuṇḍalī sahasā bhavet | tadā sā maraṇāvasthā jāyate dvipuṭāśrayā

### 3.13 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-3-13"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high · _restricted: summary only_

Concludes the account of mahāmudrā with the text's own caution about the release of the breath: 'śanaiḥ śanaireva recayennaiva vegataḥ' (only slowly, never with force); says it was shown by the great adepts; summary only.

> tataḥ śanaiḥ śanaireva recayennaiva vegataḥ | iyaṃ khalu mahāmudrā mahāsiddhaiḥ pradarśitā

`text-verified` · confidence moderate · _restricted: summary only_

The text claims that mahāmudrā removes great afflictions and things such as death, and gives it that name for this reason; not reproduced as fact.

> mahākleśādayo doṣāḥ kṣīyante maraṇādayaḥ | mahāmudrāṃ ca tenaiva vadanti vibudhottamāḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: unmarked · types: practice, powers-experiences_

### 3.14-17 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-3-14-17"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

The great afflictions and the other faults, and death itself, are destroyed, so the wise call it the great seal. Practised on the moon side, it is practised again on the sun side until the numbers are equal. For its practitioner nothing is wholesome or unwholesome; all tastes become tasteless, and even terrible poison is digested like nectar; consumption, skin disease, constipation, gulma, indigestion and the rest disappear.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: advanced · types: practice, body-layers_

practices: [Mahāmudrā (the great seal)](../practices/mahamudra.md)

### 3.19-21 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-3-19-21"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Mahābandha: placing the heel of the left foot at the perineum and the right foot on the left thigh, filling with breath, setting the chin firmly on the chest, contracting the perineum and fixing the mind in the middle; holding as far as one is able, one releases the breath slowly; practised on the left side it is practised again on the right.

`skeleton` · confidence high

Mahāvedha: seated in mahābandha, having filled (with breath) with one-pointed mind, stopping the movement of the breaths with the throat seal, the hands placed evenly on the ground, one gently strikes the buttocks (on the ground); the breath leaves the two channels and throbs in the middle. The union of moon, sun and fire arises, for immortality; a death-like state appears; then one releases the breath.

### 3.37-40 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-3-37-40"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

When the tongue, turned backward, is joined to the junction of the three paths, that is khecarī, called the 'wheel of space'. The yogin who keeps the tongue upward even for half a moment is freed from poisons, disease, death and old age; for one who knows khecarī there is no disease, death, sloth, sleep, hunger, thirst or swoon; he is not afflicted by disease, not stained by karma and not bound by time.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: advanced · types: practice, powers-experiences, karma-liberation_

### 3.47-49 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-3-47-49"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

'He who daily eats the flesh of the cow and drinks the liquor of the immortals I consider of good family (kulīna); the others are destroyers of the family.' 'Cow' (go) means the tongue, and its entry into the palate is 'eating the flesh of the cow', which destroys the great sins; the essence that flows from the moon, produced by the heat of the tongue's entry, is the liquor of the immortals (amaravāruṇī).

### 3.55-60 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-3-55-60"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Uḍḍīyāna: because the breath bound by it flies up (uḍḍīyate) in the suṣumnā, yogins call it uḍḍīyāna — the great bird flies up ceaselessly. Drawing the belly back above the navel: this lock is the lion to the elephant of death. Taught by the guru as natural, practised constantly it makes even an old man young; drawing back (the region) above and below the navel with effort and practising for six months, one conquers death. It is the best of all locks; when it is firm, liberation comes naturally.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: intermediate · types: practice, sound-language_

practices: [Uḍḍīyānabandha (the flying-up lock)](../practices/uddiyana-bandha.md)

### 3.61-65 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-3-61-65"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Mūlabandha: pressing the perineum with the heel, one contracts the anus and draws the apāna upward; by contraction it forcibly makes the downward-moving apāna go up. Pressing the anus with the heel, one contracts the breath forcibly again and again so that it rises. By mūlabandha prāṇa and apāna, nāda and bindu become one and give success in yoga; with the union of apāna and prāṇa urine and faeces diminish, and even an old man becomes young through constant mūlabandha.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: intermediate · types: practice, body-layers_

### 3.66-69 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-3-66-69"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

When apāna turns upward and reaches the region of fire, the flame, struck by the breath, grows long; then fire and apāna reach prāṇa, hot by nature; by this the fire in the body blazes intensely, and the sleeping kuṇḍalinī, heated, awakens, and like a serpent struck with a stick hisses and straightens, and enters the channel of Brahman as if into a hole; therefore yogins should always practise mūlabandha.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: body-breath, meditation · stage: advanced · types: body-layers, powers-experiences, practice_

_Superseded by [3.66](hatha-yoga-pradipika.md#tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-3-66)_

### 3.70-73 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-3-70-73"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Jālandhara — contracting the throat and pressing the chin firmly to the chest — destroys old age and death; it binds the network of channels and the downward-flowing celestial water; with it the nectar does not fall into the fire and the breath is not disturbed; by the throat contraction the two channels are stopped; this central cakra binds the sixteen supports (ādhāra).

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: all · types: practice, body-layers_

`skeleton` · confidence high

Contracting the root, performing uḍḍiyāna and binding iḍā and piṅgalā, one should make the breath flow in the rear (central) path; by this the breath dissolves and death, old age and disease do not arise; this set of three locks, practised by the great Siddhas, is known by yogins as the means of all haṭha practice.

### 3.77-79 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-3-77-79"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Whatever nectar of divine form drips from the moon is all swallowed by the sun, and so the body grows old. There is a divine practice (karaṇa) that cheats the mouth of the sun, to be learned from the guru's instruction, not from ten million śāstras: above the navel and below the palate, the sun above and the moon below — the practice called viparītakaraṇī is obtained from the guru's words.

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: body-breath · stage: all · types: body-layers, death-dying, teacher-transmission, practice_

`skeleton` · confidence high

### 3.83-103 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-3-83-103"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high · _restricted: summary only_

Vajrolī, with its variants sahajolī and amarolī: the text claims that even one living as he pleases, without the rules of yoga, becomes a vessel of success by knowing vajrolī, and treats it as a means of retaining and drawing up bindu (and, for a woman, rajas), leading to bodily perfection. It says the practice requires two things hard to obtain, and that it succeeds only for the virtuous, steadfast, seers of truth and those free of envy (3.95). It reports amarolī as a practice of the Kāpālikas of the Khaṇḍa school. RESTRICTED (sexual rite): summary only.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: advanced · types: practice_

So the knower of yoga should preserve the bindu and conquer death: death comes by the fall of bindu, life by holding it; while bindu is steady in the body, whence the fear of time?

### 3.89-90 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-3-89-90"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

From holding bindu a pleasant fragrance arises in the yogin's body; as long as bindu is steady in the body, whence the fear of death? The semen of men depends on the mind and life depends on semen; therefore both semen and mind are to be guarded with effort.

> sugandho yogino dehe jāyate bindudhāraṇāt | yāvad binduḥ sthiro dehe tāvat kālabhayaṃ kutaḥ || cittāyattaṃ nṛṇāṃ śukraṃ śukrāyattaṃ ca jīvitam | tasmācchukraṃ manaścaiva rakṣaṇīyaṃ prayatnataḥ

### 3.105-108 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-3-105-108"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

As one would force open a door with a key, so the yogin should break open the door of liberation with kuṇḍalinī. The supreme goddess sleeps covering with her mouth the door of the path by which one goes to the stainless abode of Brahman. Kuṇḍalinī sleeps above the bulb (kanda): for yogins she is for liberation, for fools for bondage; he who knows her knows yoga; he by whom that coiled śakti is moved is liberated.

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: body-breath · stage: all · types: body-layers, karma-liberation_

By moving her daily the yogin is freed from diseases; the one who has moved the power is a vessel of success and conquers time as if in play. What other means is there to cleanse the seventy-two thousand channels except the practice of kuṇḍalinī?

`skeleton` · confidence moderate

The middle channel becomes straight for yogins through firm practice of posture, breath-control and the mudrās. For the vigilant whose mind is held in samādhi, the rudrāṇī or supreme mudrā gives auspicious success. Without rājayoga the earth, the night and the mudrās, however wonderful, do not shine. One should practise the whole method of the breath with the mind engaged, and not let the mind wander elsewhere.

### 4.5 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-5"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

As salt goes to sameness (sāmya) with the water it is put into, so the oneness of the self (ātman) and the mind is called samādhi.

> salile saindhavaṃ yadvat sāmyaṃ bhajati yogataḥ | tathātmamanasoraikyaṃ samādhirabhidhīyate

_level: unmarked · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind, ultimate_

terms: [samādhi (as the HYP defines it)](../terms/samadhi-hyp.md) · concepts: [The synonyms of samādhi (HYP 4.3-4)](../concepts/hyp-samadhi-synonyms.md)

### 4.5-7 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-5-7"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

As salt dissolved in water becomes one with it, so the union of self and mind is called samādhi. When the breath is exhausted and the mind dissolved, the resulting sameness (samarasatva) is called samādhi. That sameness, the union of the individual self and the supreme self in which all intentions (saṅkalpa) have ceased, is called samādhi.

_level: ultimate · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation, body-breath · stage: realized · types: consciousness-mind, ultimate_

terms: [samādhi](../terms/samadhi.md), [samarasa(tva)](../terms/samarasa.md) · concepts: [Rājayoga as the goal and its synonyms](../concepts/rajayoga-goal.md), [The bond of mind and breath](../concepts/mind-breath-bond.md)

_Superseded by [4.5](hatha-yoga-pradipika.md#tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-5)_

### 4.6 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-6"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

When the breath (prāṇa) is exhausted and the mind dissolves, then a state of sameness of taste (samarasatva) arises, and that is called samādhi.

> yadā saṅkṣīyate prāṇo mānasaṃ ca praleyate | tadā samarasatvaṃ ca samādhirabhidhīyate

_level: unmarked · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind, ultimate_

terms: [samādhi (as the HYP defines it)](../terms/samadhi-hyp.md), [laya (as the HYP uses it)](../terms/laya-hyp.md)

### 4.7 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-7"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

That sameness is the oneness of the individual self (jīvātman) and the supreme self (paramātman): samādhi is said to be that, with all resolves (saṅkalpa) destroyed.

> tatsamaṃ ca dvayoraikyaṃ jīvātmaparamātmanoḥ | pranaṣṭasarvasaṅkalpaḥ samādhiḥ so'bhidhīyate

_level: unmarked · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind, ultimate_

### 4.8 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-8"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Who really knows the greatness of rājayoga? Knowledge, liberation, steadiness and accomplishment are obtained through the guru's word.

> rājayogasya māhātmyaṃ ko vā jānāti tattvataḥ | jñānaṃ muktiḥ sthitiḥ siddhiḥ guruvākyena labhyate

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: all · types: teacher-transmission, karma-liberation_

### 4.8-9 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-8-9"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Who truly knows the greatness of rājayoga? Knowledge, liberation, stability and success are obtained through the guru's word. Renunciation of sense-objects is hard to obtain, the vision of reality is hard, and the natural state (sahajāvasthā) is hard to obtain without the compassion of a true guru.

> rājayogasya māhātmyaṃ ko vā jānāti tattvataḥ | jñānaṃ muktiḥ sthitiḥ siddhiḥ guruvākyena labhyate || durlabho viṣayatyāgo durlabhaṃ tattvadarśanam | durlabhā sahajāvasthā sadguroḥ karuṇāṃ vinā

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath, general · stage: all · types: teacher-transmission, karma-liberation_

terms: [sahaja](../terms/sahaja.md) · concepts: [The guru in haṭha](../concepts/guru-in-hatha.md), [The innate (sahaja)](../concepts/sahaja.md) · disputes: [Is kuṇḍalinī — and the absorption it leads to — awakened by the yogin's effortful practice or by grace: the guru's, or the Lord's descent of power (śaktipāta)?](../debates/kundalini-effort-grace.md)

`text-verified` · confidence high

Hard to gain are the renunciation of objects, the vision of truth and the natural state (sahajāvasthā), without the compassion of the true guru.

> durlabho viṣayatyāgo durlabhaṃ tattvadarśanam | durlabhā sahajāvasthā sadguroḥ karuṇāṃ vinā

### 4.10 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-10"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

By diverse postures, retentions and varied practices, when the great śakti is awakened, the breath dissolves in the void.

> vividhairāsanaiḥ kumbhaiḥ vicitraiḥ karaṇairapi | prabuddhāyāṃ mahāśaktau prāṇaḥ śūnye pralīyate

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: all · types: practice, powers-experiences, body-layers_

terms: [kuṇḍalinī](../terms/kundalini.md), [śūnya (as the HYP uses it)](../terms/sunya-hyp.md)

### 4.10-12 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-10-12"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

When the great śakti is awakened by the various postures, retentions and wonderful karaṇas, the breath dissolves in the void. For the yogin whose śakti is awakened and who has abandoned all action, the natural state arises of itself. When the breath flows in suṣumnā and the mind enters the void, the knower of yoga uproots all karmas.

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: body-breath, meditation · stage: advanced · types: karma-liberation, consciousness-mind_

`text-verified` · confidence low

Salutation to you, the deathless (amara): Kāla (time, death) too has been conquered by you, in whose mouth this whole world of moving and unmoving things has fallen.

> amarāya namastubhyaṃ so'pi kālastvayā jitaḥ | patitaṃ vadane yasya jagadetaccarāram

`text-verified` · confidence low · _restricted: summary only_

The text says that when the mind has reached sameness and the breath goes in the middle, amarolī, vajrolī and sahajolī 'arise'; the three names belong to the restricted group of bodily practices (see 3.83-103), and nothing more is recorded here.

> citte samatvamāpanne vāyau vrajati madhyame | tadāmarolī vajrolī sahajolī prajāyate

### 4.15 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-15"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

How can knowledge arise in the mind as long as the breath lives and the mind does not die? The man who brings both breath and mind to dissolution (vilaya) goes to liberation (mokṣa), and no one else in any way.

> jñānaṃ kuto manasi sambhavatīha tāvat prāṇo'pi jīvati mano mriyate na yāvat | prāṇo mano dvayamidaṃ vilayaṃ nayed yo mokṣaṃ sa gacchati naro na kathañcidanyaḥ

`text-verified` · confidence low · _restricted: summary only_

The text says that, having known the true piercing (sadbheda) of suṣumnā and made the breath go in the middle, staying always in the right place, one should restrain it in the brahmarandhra; summary only.

> jñātvā suṣumnāsadbhedaṃ kṛtvā vāyuṃ ca madhyagam | sthitvā sadaiva susthāne brahmarandhre nirodhayet

### 4.17 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-17"></a>

> sūryācandramasau dhattaḥ kālaṃ rātrindivātmakam | bhoktrī suṣumnā kālasya guhyametadudāhṛtam

### 4.18 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-18"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Of the seventy-two thousand channel-doors in the body-cage, only suṣumnā, the śāmbhavī śakti, is fruitful; the rest are without purpose.

> dvāsaptatisahasrāṇi nāḍīdvārāṇi pañjare | suṣumnā śāmbhavī śaktiḥ śeṣāstveva nirarthakāḥ

`text-verified` · confidence moderate

When the breath flows in suṣumnā, manonmanī is surely attained; other practices are only toil for yogins. (The e-text places before this line a prose gloss on the previous verse, given as a note.)

> vāyuḥ iti | yasmāt paricitaḥ abhyastaḥ vāyuḥ tasmādagninā jaṭharāgninā saha kuṇḍalīṃ śaktiṃ bodhayitvā anirodhato'pratibandhāt suṣumnāyāṃ sarasvatyāṃ praviśet, vāyoḥ suṣumnāpraveśārthamabhyāsaḥ kartavyaḥ ityarthaḥ | suṣumnāvāhini prāṇe sidhyatyeva manonmanī | anyathātvitarābhyāsāḥ prayāsāyaiva yoginām

### 4.21 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-21"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

By whom the breath is bound, by him the mind is bound; by whom the mind is bound, by him the breath is bound.

> pavano badhyate yena manastenaiva badhyate | manaśca badhyate yena pavanastena badhyate

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: meditation · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind_

concepts: [Mind and breath move together (HYP)](../concepts/hyp-mind-breath-linkage.md)

### 4.21-25 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-21-25"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

The one who binds the breath binds the mind, and the one who binds the mind binds the breath. The mind has two causes, latent impressions (vāsanā) and breath; if one is destroyed both are destroyed. Where the mind dissolves the breath dissolves, and where the breath dissolves the mind dissolves. Mind and breath are mixed like milk and water and act alike; where the breath moves the mind moves. When one is destroyed the other is destroyed; while they are not destroyed the senses are active; when both are destroyed the state of liberation is attained.

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: body-breath, meditation · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind, karma-liberation_

terms: [vāsanā](../terms/vasana.md), [prāṇa](../terms/prana.md), [laya](../terms/laya.md) · concepts: [The bond of mind and breath](../concepts/mind-breath-bond.md)

_Superseded by [4.21](hatha-yoga-pradipika.md#tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-21)_

### 4.22 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-22"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Mind has two causes, latent impression (vāsanā) and breath; when one of the two is destroyed both perish.

> hetudvayaṃ ca cittasya vāsanā ca samīraṇaḥ | tayoḥ vinaṣṭa ekasmin tau dvāvapi vinaśyataḥ

### 4.23 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-23"></a>

Where the mind dissolves, the breath dissolves there; where the breath dissolves, the mind dissolves there.

> mato yatra vilīyeta pavanastatra līyate | pavano līyate yatra manastatra vilīyate

### 4.24 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-24"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Mind and breath are mixed like milk and water and have the same activity: where the breath goes, there the mind acts; where the mind goes, there the breath acts.

> dugdhāmbuvat sammilitāvubhau tulyakriyau mānasamārutau hi | yato marut tatra manaḥ pravṛttiḥ yato manastatra marutpravṛttiḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: meditation · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind_

concepts: [Mind and breath move together (HYP)](../concepts/hyp-mind-breath-linkage.md)

### 4.25 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-25"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

With the destruction of the one comes the destruction of the other, with the activity of the one the activity of the other; when the two are not destroyed the activity of the group of senses goes on, and when both are destroyed the state of liberation (mokṣapada) is accomplished.

> tatraikanāśādaparasya nāśa ekapravṛtteraparapravṛttiḥ | adhvastayoścendriyavargavṛttiḥ pradhvastayoḥ mokṣapadasya siddhiḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: meditation · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind, karma-liberation_

### 4.26 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-26"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence low · _restricted: summary only_

The text compares the mind's natural restlessness with that of mercury (rasa), and says that when mercury is bound and the mind is bound, what is not accomplished on earth? Restricted (mercury); summary only, a comparison, no method.

> rasasya manasaścaiva cañcalatvaṃ svabhāvataḥ | raso baddho mano baddhaṃ kiṃ na sidhyati bhūtale

### 4.26-27 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-26-27"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence moderate

Mercury (rasa) and the mind are by nature unsteady; when mercury is bound and the mind bound, what cannot be achieved on earth? Mercury and breath, O Pārvatī: 'swooned' they remove diseases, 'killed' they themselves give life, and 'bound' they give the power of moving in the sky.

> rasasya manasaścaiva cañcalatvaṃ svabhāvataḥ | raso baddho mano baddhaṃ kiṃ na sidhyati bhūtale || mūrcchato harate vyādhīn mṛto jīvayati svayam | baddhaḥ khecaratāṃ dhatte raso vāyuśca pārvatī

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: body-breath · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind, powers-experiences_

`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Let this alone be called liberation, or not, in another school's view (matāntare); some bliss arises when the mind and breath dissolve.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: all · types: karma-liberation, dispute_
