### 4.30-34 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-30-34"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Let this be called liberation or not (as other schools hold): when mind and breath are dissolved an indescribable bliss arises. With inhalation and exhalation ceased, the grasping of objects destroyed, motionless and changeless, the laya of yogins prevails; all intentions cut off, all activity ended, a laya known only to oneself, beyond speech. What is the mark of laya? Laya is the forgetting of objects because latent impressions no longer arise.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind, powers-experiences_

terms: [laya](../terms/laya.md), [vāsanā](../terms/vasana.md) · concepts: [Dissolution of breath and mind (laya)](../concepts/laya-natha.md)

### 4.31 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-31"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

> praṇaṣṭaśvāsaniśvāsaḥ pradhvastaviṣayagrahaḥ | niṣceṣṭo nirvikāraśca layo jayati yoginām

### 4.35-36 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-35-36"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

The Vedas, śāstras and Purāṇas are like common courtesans, but the śāmbhavī mudrā alone is guarded like a woman of good family. With attention on an inner target and the gaze outward, without blinking — that is śāmbhavī mudrā, hidden in the Vedas and śāstras.

> vedaśāstrapurāṇāni sāmānyagaṇikā iva | ekaiva śāmbhavī mudrā guptā kulavadhūriva || antarlakṣyaṃ bahirdṛṣṭiḥ nimeṣonmeṣavarjitā | eṣā sā śāmbhavī mudrā vedaśāstreṣu gopitā

### 4.43-45 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-43-45"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence moderate

When the breath stays in the middle between the left and right channels, khecarī mudrā is established there without doubt; if one swallows the breath in the void between iḍā and piṅgalā, khecarī is established; the mudrā that abides in the wheel of space, in the supportless between sun and moon, is called khecarī.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath, meditation · stage: advanced · types: practice_

practices: [Khecarī mudrā ('moving in space')](../practices/khecari-mudra.md)

### 4.43-47 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-43-47"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Khecarī (in its inner sense): when the breath moves in the middle between the left and right channels, khecarī mudrā is established there; it is established where the void between iḍā and piṅgalā swallows the breath; the mudrā situated in the space-wheel (vyomacakra) between sun and moon, in the supportless, is called khecarī; practised, khecarī gives rise to unmanī.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: body-breath, meditation · stage: advanced · types: practice, body-layers_

terms: [khecarī](../terms/khecari.md), [vyoman](../terms/vyoma.md) · concepts: [Suṣumnā, the central channel (HYP)](../concepts/susumna-central-channel.md), [Unmanī / manonmanī — the state beyond mind](../concepts/unmani.md) · practices: [Khecarī mudrā ('moving in space')](../practices/khecari-mudra.md)

### 4.48-49 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-48-49"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Between the brows is the place of Śiva; there the mind dissolves; that state is to be known as the fourth (turya), where time does not exist. One should practise khecarī until one is in yogic sleep (yoganidrā); for one who has attained yogic sleep there is never time.

> bhruvoḥ madhye śivasthānaṃ manaḥ tatra vilīyate | jñātavyaṃ tatpadaṃ turyaṃ tatra kālo na vidyate || abhyaset khecarīṃ tāvat yāvat syāt yoganidritaḥ | samprāptayoganidrasya kālo nāsti kadācana

### 4.55-56 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-55-56"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Put the self in the midst of space and space in the midst of the self; making everything space, think of nothing. Empty within, empty without, empty like a pot in space; full within, full without, full like a pot in the ocean.

_level: bridging · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind, practice_

_Superseded by [4.55](hatha-yoga-pradipika.md#tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-55)_

### 4.55-57 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-55-57"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Place the self in space and space in the self; making everything space, think of nothing at all. Empty within, empty without, empty like a pot in space; full within, full without, like a pot in the ocean. Neither outer nor inner thought should be entertained; abandoning all thought, think of nothing.

_level: ultimate · standpoint: apophatic · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind, practice_

terms: [śūnya](../terms/sunya.md) · concepts: [The void (śūnya) in Nāth teaching](../concepts/sunya-natha.md)

### 4.56 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-56"></a>

Void within, void without, void like a pot in the sky; full within, full without, full like a pot in the sky.

concepts: [Contemplations that dissolve the mind (HYP 4.50-62)](../concepts/hyp-mind-dissolution-contemplation.md)

### 4.58 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-58"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The whole world is only a fabrication of resolve (saṅkalpa), and the mind's play is only a fabrication of resolve; giving up the very idea of resolve and relying on the non-conceptual (nirvikalpa) and on certainty, attain peace, Rāma.

> saṅkalpamātrakalanaiva jagat samagraṃ saṅkalpamātrakalanaiva mano vilāsaḥ | saṅkalpamātramatimutsṛjya nirvikalpaṃ āśritya niścayamapānuhi rāma śāntim

_level: ultimate · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation, knowledge · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind, karma-liberation, ultimate_

### 4.59 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-59"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

As camphor in fire and rock-salt in water, so the mind, brought together with it, dissolves in reality (tattva).

> karpūramanale yadvat saindhavaṃ salile yathā | tathā sandhīyamānaṃ tu manastattve vilīyate

_level: unmarked · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind_

concepts: [Contemplations that dissolve the mind (HYP 4.50-62)](../concepts/hyp-mind-dissolution-contemplation.md)

### 4.59-62 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-59-62"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

As camphor in fire and salt in water, so the mind joined with reality dissolves. The knowable, all that is perceived, and knowing are called mind; when knower and known are equally destroyed there is no second path. This whole world of moving and unmoving things is duality seen by the mind; through the mind's state of unmanī duality is not perceived. By abandoning the knowable the mind dissolves; when the mind is dissolved, isolation (kaivalya) remains.

_level: ultimate · standpoint: absolute · path: meditation, knowledge · stage: realized · types: consciousness-mind, karma-liberation, ultimate_

terms: [unmanī](../terms/unmani.md), [kaivalya](../terms/kaivalya.md) · concepts: [Unmanī / manonmanī — the state beyond mind](../concepts/unmani.md), [Kaivalya (YS 3.50, 3.55, 4.26-4.34)](../concepts/kaivalya.md), [Dissolution of breath and mind (laya)](../concepts/laya-natha.md)

_Superseded by [4.59](hatha-yoga-pradipika.md#tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-59)_

### 4.60 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-60"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

All that is to be known, and its being known, and knowing itself are called mind; when knowing and the known are both destroyed together there is no second path.

> jñeyaṃ sarvaṃ pratītaṃ ca jñānaṃ ca mana ucyate | jñānaṃ jñeyaṃ samaṃ naṣṭaṃ nānyaḥ panthā dvitīyakaḥ

_level: ultimate · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind, ultimate_

concepts: [Contemplations that dissolve the mind (HYP 4.50-62)](../concepts/hyp-mind-dissolution-contemplation.md)

### 4.61 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-61"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

This duality, all that is moving or unmoving, is seen by the mind; through the mind's becoming unmanī duality is not perceived.

> manodṛśyamidaṃ dvaitaṃ yatkiñcit sacarācaram | manaso hyunmanībhāvāt dvaitaṃ naivopalabhyate

_level: ultimate · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind, ultimate_

### 4.62 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-62"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

By giving up objects of knowledge the mind dissolves; when the mind has dissolved, kaivalya remains.

> jñeyavastuparityāgāt vilayaṃ yāti mānasam | manaso vilaye jāte kaivalyamavaśiṣyate

_level: ultimate · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind, karma-liberation_

### 4.63 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-63"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Thus various means, each well joined with one's own experience, have been taught as paths to samādhi by earlier great teachers (pūrvācārya).

> evaṃ nānāvidhopāyāḥ samyak svānubhavanvitāḥ | samādhimārgāḥ kathitāḥ pūrvācāryaiḥ mahātmabhiḥ

`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Salutation to suṣumnā, kuṇḍalī, nectar (sudhā), the moon-born, manonmanī, the great śakti whose nature is consciousness.

> suṣumnāyai kuṇḍalyai sudhāyai candrajanmane | manonmanyai namastubhyaṃ mahāśaktyai cidātmane

### 4.65 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-65"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Nādopāsana (worship of the inner sound) was taught by Gorakṣanātha, and is accepted even for those unable to grasp the truth, even for the dull.

> aśakyatattvabodhānāṃ mūḍhānāmapi sammatam | proktaṃ gorakṣanāthena nādopāsanamucyate

### 4.65-66 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-65-66"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

The worship of the inner sound (nādopāsana) taught by Gorakṣanātha is now told, approved even for the dull who cannot grasp reality. Ādinātha taught a crore and a quarter ways of laya, and they prevail; but we consider attention to the inner sound (nādānusandhāna) alone the chief of the layas.

> aśakyatattvabodhānāṃ mūḍhānāmapi sammatam | proktaṃ gorakṣanāthena nādopāsanamucyate || śrīādināthena sapādakoṭilayaprakārāḥ kathitā jayanti | nādānusandhānakamekameva manyāmahe mukhyatamaṃ layānām

`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Seated in muktāsana, having taken up śāmbhavī mudrā, one should listen with one-pointed mind to the inner sound (nāda) in the right ear.

> muktāsane sthito yogī mudrāṃ sandhāya śāmbhavīm | śruṇuyād dakṣiṇe karṇe nādamantasthamekadhīḥ

### 4.67-68 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-67-68"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Seated in muktāsana, adopting śāmbhavī mudrā, the yogin should listen with one-pointed mind to the sound within, in the right ear. Closing the ears, eyes, nose and mouth, a clear, pure sound is heard in the purified path of suṣumnā.

> muktāsane sthito yogī mudrāṃ sandhāya śāmbhavīm | śruṇuyād dakṣiṇe karṇe nādamantasthamekadhīḥ || śravaṇapuṭanayanayugalaghrāṇamukhānāṃ nirodhanaṃ kāryam | śuddhasuṣumnā saraṇau sphuṭamamalaḥ śrūyate nādaḥ

### 4.69 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-69"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

In all yogas there is a fourfold state: beginning (ārambha), the pot (ghaṭa), acquaintance (paricaya) and accomplishment (niṣpatti).

> ārambhaśca ghaṭaścaiva tathā paricayo'pi ca | niṣpattiḥ sarvayogeṣu syādavasthācatuṣṭayam

### 4.70 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-70"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The beginning state (ārambha): the knot of Brahmā is pierced, bliss arises from the void, and various tinkling sounds and the unstruck (anāhata) sound are heard in the body.

> athārambhāvasthā - brahmagantheḥ bhavedbhedo hyānandaḥ śūnyasambhavaḥ | vicitraḥ kvāṇako dehe'nāhataḥ śrūyate dhvaniḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: all · types: powers-experiences_

terms: [nāda](../terms/nada.md) · concepts: [The four states of the yoga of inner sound (ārambha, ghaṭa, paricaya, niṣpatti)](../concepts/hyp-four-avasthas.md)

### 4.70-71 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-70-71"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

The beginning stage: when the knot of Brahmā is pierced, bliss arising from the void and various tinkling sounds, the unstruck sound, are heard in the body; in the beginning stage the yogin has a divine body, radiant, divinely fragrant, free of disease, with a full heart, in the void.

> brahmagantheḥ bhavedbhedo hyānandaḥ śūnyasambhavaḥ | vicitraḥ kvāṇako dehe'nāhataḥ śrūyate dhvaniḥ || divyadehaśca tejasvī divyagandhastvarogavān | sampūrṇahṛdayaḥ śūnya ārambhe yogavān bhavet

### 4.71 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-71"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The text says that in the beginning state (ārambha) the yogin has a divine, radiant body with a divine fragrance, is free of disease, has a full heart and is in the void.

> divyadehaśca tejasvī divyagandhastvarogavān | sampūrṇahṛdayaḥ śūnya ārambhe yogavān bhavet

### 4.72 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-72"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The state of the pot (ghaṭa): in the second the breath becomes middle-going, the yogin becomes firm in posture, wise and equal to the gods.

> atha ghaṭāvasthā - dvitīyāyāṃ ghaṭīkṛtya vāyurbhavati madhyagaḥ | dṛḍhāsano bhavedyogī jñānī devasamastadā

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: all · types: powers-experiences_

terms: [suṣumnā](../terms/susumna.md) · concepts: [The four states of the yoga of inner sound (ārambha, ghaṭa, paricaya, niṣpatti)](../concepts/hyp-four-avasthas.md)

### 4.72-73 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-72-73"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

In the second stage, the breath, united into one (ghaṭīkṛtya), goes into the middle; the yogin's posture is firm, he is wise, like a god. Then, from the piercing of the knot of Viṣṇu, the sound of a kettledrum arises in the extreme void (atiśūnya), indicating supreme bliss.

> dvitīyāyāṃ ghaṭīkṛtya vāyurbhavati madhyagaḥ | dṛḍhāsano bhavedyogī jñānī devasamastadā || viṣṇugrantheḥ tato bhedāt paramānandasūcakaḥ | atiśūnye vimardaśca bherīśabdastadā bhavet

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: sound, meditation, body-breath · stage: intermediate (ghaṭa) · types: powers-experiences, sound-language_

terms: [ghaṭa](../terms/ghata.md), [viṣṇugranthi](../terms/visnu-granthi.md) · concepts: [The four stages of yoga (ārambha, ghaṭa, paricaya, niṣpatti)](../concepts/four-avasthas-of-yoga.md), [The three knots (granthi)](../concepts/three-granthis.md), [The void (śūnya) in Nāth teaching](../concepts/sunya-natha.md)

_Superseded by [4.72](hatha-yoga-pradipika.md#tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-72)_

### 4.73 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-73"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Then, by the piercing of the knot of Viṣṇu, (a sound) that signals supreme bliss arises, and in the atiśūnya (the 'beyond-void') a clashing (vimarda) and the sound of a kettledrum (bherī).

> viṣṇugrantheḥ tato bhedāt paramānandasūcakaḥ | atiśūnye vimardaśca bherīśabdastadā bhavet

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: all · types: powers-experiences_

terms: [nāda](../terms/nada.md) · concepts: [The four states of the yoga of inner sound (ārambha, ghaṭa, paricaya, niṣpatti)](../concepts/hyp-four-avasthas.md)

### 4.74 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-74"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

In the third state the sound of the mardala drum is known in the sky; then he goes to the great void, the support of all accomplishments.

> tṛtīyāyāṃ tu vijñeyo vihāyomardaladhvaniḥ | mahāśūnyaṃ tadā yāti sarvasiddhisamāśrayam

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: all · types: powers-experiences_

terms: [nāda](../terms/nada.md) · concepts: [The four states of the yoga of inner sound (ārambha, ghaṭa, paricaya, niṣpatti)](../concepts/hyp-four-avasthas.md)

### 4.74-75 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-74-75"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

In the third stage the sound of a drum (mardala) is heard in the space (between the brows); the breath goes to the great void (mahāśūnya), the abode of all powers. Having conquered the bliss of mind, natural bliss (sahajānanda) arises, free of faults, pain, old age, disease, hunger and sleep.

> tṛtīyāyāṃ tu vijñeyo vihāyomardaladhvaniḥ | mahāśūnyaṃ tadā yāti sarvasiddhisamāśrayam || cittānandaṃ tadā jitvā sahajānandasambhavaḥ | doṣaduḥkhajarāvyādhikṣudhānidrāvivarjitaḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: sound, meditation, body-breath · stage: intermediate (paricaya) · types: powers-experiences, sound-language_

terms: [paricaya](../terms/paricaya.md), [sahaja](../terms/sahaja.md) · concepts: [The four stages of yoga (ārambha, ghaṭa, paricaya, niṣpatti)](../concepts/four-avasthas-of-yoga.md), [The void (śūnya) in Nāth teaching](../concepts/sunya-natha.md), [The innate (sahaja)](../concepts/sahaja.md)

_Superseded by [4.74](hatha-yoga-pradipika.md#tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-74)_

### 4.75 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-75"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Then, having conquered mind-bliss, the bliss of the natural state (sahajānanda) arises, and the yogin is free of fault, sorrow, old age, disease, hunger and sleep, the text says.

> cittānandaṃ tadā jitvā sahajānandasambhavaḥ | doṣaduḥkhajarāvyādhikṣudhānidrāvivarjitaḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: all · types: powers-experiences_

### 4.76 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-76"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

When the knot of Rudra is pierced and the breath reaches the seat of Śarva, in accomplishment (niṣpatti) the sound of a flute and of a vīṇā arises.

> rudragranthiṃ yadā bhittvā śarvapīṭhagato'nilaḥ | niṣpattau vaiṇavaḥ śabdaḥ kvaṇadvīṇākvaṇo bhavet

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: all · types: powers-experiences_

terms: [nāda](../terms/nada.md) · concepts: [The four states of the yoga of inner sound (ārambha, ghaṭa, paricaya, niṣpatti)](../concepts/hyp-four-avasthas.md)

### 4.76-77 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-76-77"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

When the knot of Rudra is pierced and the breath reaches the seat of Śiva (śarvapīṭha), in the consummation stage a sound like a flute and the resonance of a vīṇā arise. Then the mind becomes one — this is called rājayoga; the yogin, creator and destroyer, becomes equal to the Lord.

> rudragranthiṃ yadā bhittvā śarvapīṭhagato'nilaḥ | niṣpattau vaiṇavaḥ śabdaḥ kvaṇadvīṇākvaṇo bhavet || ekībhūtaṃ tadā cittaṃ rājayogābhidhānakam | sṛṣṭisaṃhārakartāsau yogīśvarasamo bhavet

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: sound, meditation, body-breath · stage: realized (niṣpatti) · types: powers-experiences, sound-language, karma-liberation_

terms: [niṣpatti](../terms/nispatti.md), [rudragranthi](../terms/rudra-granthi.md), [rājayoga](../terms/raja-yoga.md) · concepts: [The four stages of yoga (ārambha, ghaṭa, paricaya, niṣpatti)](../concepts/four-avasthas-of-yoga.md), [The three knots (granthi)](../concepts/three-granthis.md), [Rājayoga as the goal and its synonyms](../concepts/rajayoga-goal.md)

_Superseded by [4.76](hatha-yoga-pradipika.md#tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-76)_

### 4.77 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-77"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Then the mind becomes one; this is called rājayoga; the yogin becomes the creator and destroyer, like the lord of yogins.

> ekībhūtaṃ tadā cittaṃ rājayogābhidhānakam | sṛṣṭisaṃhārakartāsau yogīśvarasamo bhavet

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: all · types: powers-experiences, ultimate_

### 4.78 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-78"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Let there be liberation or not; here there is unbroken happiness; this bliss born of laya is obtained through rājayoga.

> astu vā māstu vā muktiratraivākhaṇḍitaṃ sukham | layodbhavamidaṃ saukhyaṃ rājayogādavāpyate

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: all · types: karma-liberation, dispute, consciousness-mind_

terms: [rājayoga](../terms/raja-yoga.md), [laya (as the HYP uses it)](../terms/laya-hyp.md) · concepts: [Dissolution of breath and mind (laya)](../concepts/laya-natha.md), [Rājayoga as the goal and its synonyms](../concepts/rajayoga-goal.md) · disputes: [Is the bliss of laya (dissolution of mind and breath) to be called liberation?](../debates/hyp-is-laya-liberation.md)

### 4.79 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-79"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Those who do not know rājayoga and are only haṭha-workers (haṭhakarmin): the speaker thinks these practitioners are without the fruit of their toil.

> rājayogamajānantaḥ kevalaṃ haṭhakarmiṇaḥ | etānabhyāsino manye prayāsaphalavarjitān

_level: conventional · standpoint: polemical · path: body-breath, meditation · stage: all · types: practice, dispute_

terms: [rājayoga](../terms/raja-yoga.md), [haṭha](../terms/hatha.md) · concepts: [Haṭha and rājayoga depend on each other](../concepts/hatha-raja-interdependence.md), [Rājayoga as the goal and its synonyms](../concepts/rajayoga-goal.md) · disputes: [Is haṭha a necessary means to rājayoga, and has it any value apart from it?](../debates/hatha-vs-raja.md)

### 4.80 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-80"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Unmanī is quickly obtained; contemplation between the brows is accepted by the speaker as an easy means for the dull to reach the state of rājayoga; laya born of nāda gives immediate confirmation (pratyaya).

> unmanyavāpyate śīghraṃ bhrūdhyānaṃ mama sammatam | rājayogapadaṃ prāptuṃ sukhopāyo'lpacetasām | sadyaḥ pratyayasandhāyī jāyate nādajo layaḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation, sound · stage: all · types: practice, consciousness-mind_

### 4.81 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-81"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The bliss growing in the heart of the lords of yoga who are absorbed in attention to the inner sound, which words cannot reach, is known only by the glorious guru-nātha.

> nādānusandhānasamādhibhājāṃ yogīśvarāṇāṃ hṛdi vardhamānam | ānandamekaṃ vacasāmagamyaṃ jānāti taṃ śrīgurunātha ekaḥ

### 4.82 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-82"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Closing the ears with the hands, the sage should fix the mind on whatever sound he hears, until he reaches the steady state.

> karṇau pidhāya hastābhyāṃ yaṃ śruṇoti dhvaniṃ muniḥ | tatra cittaṃ sthirīkuryāt yāvat sthirapadaṃ vrajet

### 4.82-83 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-82-83"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

The sound the sage hears after closing his ears with his hands — on that he should fix the mind until he reaches the steady state. Practised, this sound drowns out external sound; within a fortnight, having overcome all distraction, the yogin becomes happy.

> karṇau pidhāya hastābhyāṃ yaṃ śruṇoti dhvaniṃ muniḥ | tatra cittaṃ sthirīkuryāt yāvat sthirapadaṃ vrajet || abhyasyamāno nādo'yaṃ bāhyamāvṛṇute dhvanim | pakṣād vikṣepamakhilaṃ jitvā yogī sukhī bhavet

### 4.82-84 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-82-84"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Closing the ears with the hands, the sage should fix the mind on the sound he hears until he reaches the steady state. This nāda, practised, covers outer sound; within a fortnight, overcoming all distraction, the yogin becomes happy. In the first practice many loud sounds are heard; as practice grows, subtler and subtler ones are heard.

This practised nāda covers the outer sound; within a fortnight the yogin, having conquered all distraction, becomes happy, the text says.

`text-verified` · confidence high

In the first practice many great kinds of nāda are heard; as practice grows, a very subtle nāda is heard.

> śrūyate prathāmābhyāse nādo nānāvidho mahān | tato'bhyāse vardhamāne śrūyate sūkṣmasūkṣmakaḥ

### 4.84-86 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-84-86"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

In the first practice many loud sounds are heard; as practice grows, subtler and subtler ones. At first sounds arising like the ocean, thunder-clouds, kettledrums and cymbals; in the middle like the drum (mardala), conch, bell and horn; at the end like tinkling bells, flute, vīṇā and bees — such various sounds are heard within the body.

### 4.85 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-85"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

At first the sounds are like the ocean, thunder-cloud, kettledrum and jharjhara; in the middle those of the mardala drum and conch, and those born of bell and kāhala horn.

> ādau jaladhijīmūtabherījharjharasambhavāḥ | madhye mardalaśaṅkhotthāḥ ghaṇṭākāhalajāstathā

At first sounds like the ocean, thunder-clouds, the kettle-drum and the jharjhara; in the middle, sounds arising from the mardala drum and the conch, the bell and the large horn; at the end, tinkling bells, flute, vīṇā and the hum of bees — these various sounds are heard within the body.

> ādau jaladhijīmūtabherījharjharasambhavāḥ | madhye mardalaśaṅkhotthāḥ ghaṇṭākāhalajāstathā || ante tu kiṅkiṇīvaṃśavīṇābhramaranisvanāḥ | iti nānāvidhā nādāḥ śrūyante dehamadhyagāḥ

`text-verified` · confidence high

At the end the sounds are of tiny bells, flute, vīṇā and bee; thus various kinds of nāda are heard in the middle of the body.

### 4.87 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-87"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Even while a great sound such as a cloud or kettledrum is heard, one should attend only to the subtler and still subtler nāda.

> mahati śrūyamāṇe'pi meghabheryādike dhvanau | tatra sūkṣmāt sūkṣmataraṃ nādameva parāmṛśet

### 4.87-89 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-87-89"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Even while loud sounds like thunder and kettledrum are heard, one should attend to the subtler sound; whether leaving the dense for the subtle or the subtle for the dense, the mind, though it delights and strays, should not be moved elsewhere; wherever the mind first attaches in the sound, there it becomes steady and dissolves along with it.

### 4.88 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-88"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Whether leaving the gross for the subtle or the subtle for the gross, the mind, though delighting, should not be moved to anything else.

> ghanamutsṛjya vā sūkṣme sūkṣmamutsṛjya vā ghane | ramamāṇamapi kṣiptaṃ mano nānyatra cālayet

### 4.89 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-89"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

To whatever nāda the mind first clings, there becoming steady, it dissolves along with it.

> yatra kutrāpi vā nāde lagati prathamaṃ manaḥ | tatraiva susthirībhūya tena sārdhaṃ vilīyate

### 4.90 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-90"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

As a bee drinking honey does not care for fragrance, so the mind attached to nāda does not long for objects.

> makarandaṃ piban bhṛṅgo gandhaṃ nāpekṣate yathā | nādāsaktaṃ tathā cittaṃ viṣayān na hi kāṅkṣate

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: sound · stage: all · types: practice, consciousness-mind_

### 4.90-92 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-90-92"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

As the bee drinking nectar does not care for the scent, so the mind attached to nāda does not crave sense-objects. For the mad elephant of the mind roaming in the garden of sense-objects, this nāda is a sharp goad able to restrain it. Bound by the rope of nāda, the mind abandons its restlessness and becomes very steady, like a bird with clipped wings.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: sound, meditation, body-breath · stage: intermediate · types: consciousness-mind, sound-language, practice_

### 4.91 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-91"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The mind is like a mad elephant roaming the garden of objects; the goad sharpened by nāda is able to control it.

> mano mattagajendrasya viṣayodyānacāriṇaḥ | samartho'yaṃ niyamane ninādaniśitāṅkuśa

### 4.92 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-92"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The mind bound by the bond of nāda, having given up restlessness, reaches great steadiness, like a bird with clipped wings.

> baddhaṃ tu nādabandhena manaḥ santyaktacāpalam | prayāti sutarāṃ sthairyaṃ chinnapakṣaḥ khago yathā

### 4.93 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-93"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Giving up all thought, with an attentive mind, one who wishes for the empire of yoga should attend to nāda alone.

> sarvacintāṃ parityajya sāvadhānena cetasā | nāda evānusandheyo yogasāmrājyamicchatā

practices: [Attention to the inner sound (nādānusandhāna) in the haṭha texts](../practices/nadanusandhana.md)

### 4.94 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-94"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Nāda is like a net for the deer of the inner mind, and also like a hunter for its killing.

> nādo'ntaraṅgasāraṅgabandhane vāgurāyate | antaraṅgakuraṅgasya vadhe vyādhāyate'pi ca

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: sound · stage: all · types: practice, consciousness-mind_

practices: [Attention to the inner sound (nādānusandhāna) in the haṭha texts](../practices/nadanusandhana.md)

### 4.94-98 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-94-98"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence moderate

Nāda is a snare for the deer of the inner mind and also its slayer; it is the bar for the horse of the self-controlled mind; the mercury of mind, bound by the 'sulphur' of nāda, loses its fickleness and moves in the supportless space; on hearing nāda the serpent of the mind forgets everything and runs nowhere. As fire kindled in wood is extinguished with the wood, so the mind directed to nāda dissolves together with nāda.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: body-breath, sound · stage: advanced · types: practice, consciousness-mind_

`text-verified` · confidence low

For the inner horse of the self-controlled, nāda is like a bar; devotion to worship of nāda is always to be resolved upon by the yogin.

> antaraṅgasya yaminaḥ vājinaḥ parighāyate | nādopāstirato nityamavadhāryā hi yoginā

`text-verified` · confidence low · _restricted: summary only_

Uses alchemical language as a metaphor: the mercury of the mind (manaḥpārada), bound and freed of restlessness by being digested (jāraṇa) with the 'sulphur' (gandhaka) of nāda, attains roaming in the sky called the supportless (nirālamba); restricted (mercury vocabulary), a metaphor for the mind and not an alchemical instruction.

By hearing nāda the snake of the inner mind, forgetting everything and one-pointed, does not run anywhere.

> nādaśravaṇataḥ kṣipramantaraṅgabhujaṅgamaḥ | vismṛtya sarvamekāgraḥ kutracinnahi dhāvati

Fire fed on wood is quenched with the wood; the mind engaged in nāda dissolves with nāda.

For the deer of the inner sense, bound to bell-sounds and the like, killing is easy for one skilled in aiming an arrow.

### 4.100 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-100"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

In the sound of the unstruck (anāhata) that is perceived as an echo, the known is within the echo and the mind is within the known; the mind dissolves there, that is Viṣṇu's supreme state.

> anāhatasya śabdasya dhvanirya upalabhyate | dhvanerantargataṃ jñeyaṃ jñeyasyāntargataṃ manaḥ | manastatra layaṃ yāti tadviṣṇoḥ paramaṃ padam

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: sound, body-breath · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind, powers-experiences, sound-language, ultimate_

### 4.100-102 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-100-102"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Within the sound of the unstruck (anāhata) is the resonance; within the resonance is the knowable; within the knowable is the mind; there the mind dissolves — that is the supreme abode of Viṣṇu. As long as there is sound there is the notion of space; the soundless is called the supreme Brahman, the supreme self. Whatever is heard in the form of nāda is śakti; the formless end of the tattvas is the supreme Lord (parameśvara).

_level: ultimate · standpoint: absolute · path: sound, meditation · stage: realized · types: ultimate, sound-language_

terms: [anāhata nāda](../terms/anahata-nada.md), [nāda](../terms/nada.md) · concepts: [Nāda — the inner sound](../concepts/nada.md), [Dissolution of breath and mind (laya)](../concepts/laya-natha.md)

### 4.101 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-101"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The notion of space (ākāśa) lasts only as long as sound occurs; the soundless is the supreme Brahman, called the supreme self (paramātman).

> tāvadākāśasaṅkalpo yāvacchabdaḥ pravartate | niḥśabdaṃ tatparaṃ brahma paramātmeti gīyate

`text-verified` · confidence high

All the means of haṭha and laya are for the accomplishment of rājayoga; the text says that a person established in rājayoga is a cheater of Kāla (time, death).

### 4.114 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-4-114"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The text's closing warning: as long as the moving breath does not enter the middle path, as long as bindu is not firm by binding the breath, and as long as in meditation reality does not arise like the natural state, so long is talk of 'knowledge' only 'dambhamithyāpralāpaḥ' (boasting and false prattle).

> yāvannaiva praviśati caranmāruto madhyamārge yāvad bindurna bhavati dṛḍhaḥ prāṇavātaprabandhāt | yāvad dhyāne sahajasadṛśaṃ jāyate naiva tattvam tāvajjñānaṃ vadati tadidaṃ dambhamithyāpralāpaḥ

### ch1 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-ch1"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

First upadeśa (67 segments): opens with homage to Ādinātha, the statement that haṭha is taught solely for rājayoga, and a list of the adepts of haṭha (1.1-9); praise and secrecy of haṭha (1.10-11); the fitting dwelling (1.12-14); the six that destroy and the six that further yoga (1.15-16); then posture (āsana) as the first limb: many postures, the four principal ones, especially siddhāsana (1.17-54); the sequence of haṭha practice, moderate eating and foods (1.55-63, summary only); that anyone who practises can succeed and that practice, not talk or dress, is the cause (1.64-66); and the statement that all these practices lead up to rājayoga (1.67).

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: all · types: practice_

terms: [haṭha](../terms/hatha.md), [rājayoga](../terms/raja-yoga.md) · concepts: [Conditions for practice in the Haṭhapradīpikā](../concepts/hyp-conditions-for-practice.md)

### ch2 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-ch2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Second upadeśa (76 segments): breath-control (prāṇāyāma) as the next stage after firm posture and why (2.1-6); a channel-purifying breath practice and cautions about proper measure, quoted from the text (2.7-18; summary only); signs of purified channels (2.19); the six cleansing acts and the difference of view on whether they are needed (2.21-38; summary only); the eight retentions and the kinds of retention (2.39-75; summary only); and the statement that haṭha and rājayoga depend on each other and the marks of haṭha's success (2.76-78).

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: unmarked · types: practice_

terms: [prāṇāyāma](../terms/pranayama.md), [kumbhaka](../terms/kumbhaka.md), [ṣaṭkarma](../terms/satkarma.md)

### ch3 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-ch3"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Third upadeśa (130 segments): kuṇḍalī as the support of yoga and what the text says follows her awakening by the guru's grace (3.1-4); the ten mudrās, their secrecy and the teacher's role (3.5-9, 3.128-130); the ten mudrās taken one by one (mahāmudrā 3.10-18, mahābandha 3.19-25, mahāvedha 3.26-31, khecarī 3.32-54, uḍḍīyāna 3.55-60, mūlabandha 3.61-69, jālandhara 3.70-76, viparītakaraṇī 3.77-82, vajrolī with sahajolī and amarolī 3.83-103, śakticālana 3.104-123), all summary-only in this ontology; and closing remarks on the middle channel and on the mudrās not shining without rājayoga (3.124-127).

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: unmarked · types: practice_

terms: [mudrā](../terms/mudra.md), [kuṇḍalinī](../terms/kundalini.md) · concepts: [The ten mudrās (HYP 3.6-9, 3.128)](../concepts/ten-mudras-hyp.md)

### ch4 <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-ch4"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Fourth upadeśa (114 segments): homage and the announcement of the order of samādhi (4.1-2); synonyms and definitions of samādhi and rājayoga (4.3-7); the role of the guru, of śakti and of the middle channel (4.8-20); the mutual dependence of mind and breath and laya as the lord of the breath (4.21-34); śāmbhavī and khecarī as states (4.35-49, largely summary-only); contemplations that dissolve the mind (4.50-62); closing of the survey of methods (4.63-64); nāda-attention: its method, its four stages of ārambha, ghaṭa, paricaya and niṣpatti, the inner sounds, the images of the mind as an animal (4.65-99); the soundless as the highest (4.100-102); haṭha, laya and rājayoga related and the marks of the yogin in samādhi (4.103-113); and the closing warning against empty talk of knowledge (4.114).

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: all · types: practice_

terms: [samādhi (as the HYP defines it)](../terms/samadhi-hyp.md), [nāda](../terms/nada.md), [laya (as the HYP uses it)](../terms/laya-hyp.md), [rājayoga](../terms/raja-yoga.md)

### thesis <a id="tea-hatha-yoga-pradipika-thesis"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The Haṭhapradīpikā presents its whole body of practice (posture, purification, breath practices, mudrās and attention to the inner sound) as haṭha, taught 'solely for the sake of rājayoga' (1.2), that is, for the state of samādhi in which mind and breath dissolve and the self and the mind become one (4.3-7); haṭha and rājayoga are said to depend on each other (2.76), and those who practise haṭha without knowing rājayoga are said to labour without fruit (4.79). Marks of purport (ṣaḍ-liṅga): (1) Opening and closing: the teaching opens by stating that haṭha is for rājayoga (1.2) and closes by saying that all haṭha and laya means are for its accomplishment (4.103) and by the seed-field-water image (4.104), with a last warning against empty talk of knowledge (4.114). (2) Repetition: the same relation of haṭha to rājayoga is stated at 1.2, 1.67, 2.76, 3.126, 4.79 and 4.103; the necessity of the guru and of secrecy recurs (1.11, 1.14, 2.23, 3.9, 3.78-79, 3.129, 4.8-9). (3) Novelty: the text ranks its chief items (1.43) and puts attention to the inner sound as the chief of ways of laya (4.66); it gives the teaching of the mutual dependence of mind and breath (4.21-25). (4) Stated result: rājayoga, samādhi, unmanī, the natural state (4.3-4), the yogin's marks in samādhi (4.106-113), and 'let there be liberation or not, here is unbroken happiness' (4.78). (5) Praise (arthavāda), to be read as rhetorical, not as doctrine: the great adepts who have broken the staff of Death (1.9), claims for postures (1.31, 1.47), for the mudrās (3.8, 3.16, 3.29-30), for khecarī (3.38-40), and the samādhi-yogin who cannot be slain by weapons (4.113). (6) Reasoned demonstration (upapatti): the argument from the joint movement of mind and breath (2.2, 4.21-25, 4.28-29) and the similes of salt in water and camphor in fire (4.5, 4.59).

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath, meditation · stage: all · types: practice, karma-liberation_
